On the structure of Multiple q-Zeta Values
Benjamin Brindle

Abstract
. In 2015, Bachmann [ 3 ] conjectured that the ℚ \mathbb{Q} -vector space 𝒵 q f \mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f} of (formal) q q -analogues of Multiple Zeta Values ( q q MZVs) is spanned by a very particular set compared to known spanning sets. In this work, we prove that this conjecture is true for a subspace of 𝒵 q f \mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f} spanned by words satisfying some condition on their number of zeros and depth. According to this partial result, we give an explicit approach to the whole conjecture, based on particular ℚ \mathbb{Q} -linear relations among formal Multiple q q -Zeta Values which are implied by duality.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
05A30, 11M32, 13J25.

1.
Introduction

Given a field
F
and a countable set
\mathcal{A}
, we call
\mathcal{A}
also an
alphabet
and elements of
\mathcal{A}
are referred to as
letters
. Denote by
\operatorname{span}_{F}\mathcal{A}
the
F
-vector space spanned by elements of
\mathcal{A}
. Furthermore, monomials of elements in
\mathcal{A}
(with respect to concatenation) are called
words
. Usually, the neutral element with respect to concatenation is denoted by
{\mathbf{1}}
and called the
empty word
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{\ast}
denote the set of words with letters in
\mathcal{A}
, then we write
F\langle\mathcal{A}\rangle
for the
F
-vector space
\operatorname{span}_{F}\mathcal{A}^{\ast}
, equipped with the non-commutative, but associative multiplication, given by concatenation.

Choosing
F=\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{U}:=\{u_{j}\mid j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}
, we define the
stuffle product
to be the
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear map
\ast\colon\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle\times\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle\to\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle
recursively via

\displaystyle u_{j_{1}}\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast u_{j_{2}}\mathtt{W}_{2}=u_{j_{1}}(\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast u_{j_{2}}\mathtt{W}_{2})+u_{j_{2}}(u_{j_{1}}\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2})+u_{j_{1}+j_{2}}(\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2})

for all
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\mathtt{W}_{1},\mathtt{W}_{2}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast}
with initial condition
\boldsymbol{1}\ast\mathtt{W}=\mathtt{W}\ast\boldsymbol{1}=\mathtt{W}
for any
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast}
. By Hoffman (
[
8
]
),
(\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle,\ast)
is an associative and commutative
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra. For a word
\mathtt{W}=u_{k_{1}}\cdots u_{k_{r}}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast}
, we often write
u_{\mathbf{k}}
(
u_{\emptyset}:={\mathbf{1}}
), where
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
, and associate the notion of

\displaystyle\textit{length},

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname{len}(\mathtt{W}):=\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{k}):=r,

\displaystyle\textit{depth},

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W}):=\operatorname{depth}(\mathbf{k}):=\#\{k_{j}\neq 0\mid 1\leq j\leq r\},

\displaystyle\textit{number of zeros},

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W}):=\operatorname{zero}(\mathbf{k}):=\#\{k_{j}=0\mid 1\leq j\leq r\},

\displaystyle\textit{weight},

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}):=\operatorname{wt}(\mathbf{k}):=|\mathbf{k}|+\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W}),

where
|\mathbf{k}|:=k_{1}+\cdots+k_{r}
. Furthermore, we denote
\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}:=\mathcal{U}^{\ast}\backslash u_{0}\mathcal{U}^{\ast}
to be the set of words not starting with
u_{0}
and we define the corresponding
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\subset\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle
spanned by the words from
\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
. Note that
\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
is closed under
\ast
which gives rise to a commutative
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
(\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},\ast)
(see
[
8
]
). The map
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{SZ}}\colon(\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},\ast)\to(\mathbb{Q}\llbracket q\rrbracket,\cdot)
is the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra homomorphism (see
[
7
]
) defined via
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{SZ}}({\mathbf{1}})=1
,
\mathbb{Q}
-linearity, and, with
m_{d+1}:=0
,

(0.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{SZ}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}u_{0}^{z_{d}}\right):=\sum\limits_{m_{1}>\cdots>m_{d}>0}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{d}\binom{m_{j}-m_{j+1}-1}{z_{j}}\frac{q^{m_{j}k_{j}}}{(1-q^{m_{j}})^{k_{j}}},

for any
k_{1},\dots,k_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
z_{1},\dots,z_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
where
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
(note that this definition is not the usual one, like in
[
11
]
, but equivalent to it; this statement can be deduced, e.g., from
[
5
, Theorem 2.18]
). We denote by
\mathcal{Z}_{q}
the image of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{SZ}}
and call elements in
\mathcal{Z}_{q}

(Schlesinger–Zudilin)
q
MZVs
((SZ-)
q
MZVs for short). Note that these
q
-series are
q
-analogues of Multiple Zeta Values since in the case
k_{1}\geq 2
and
z_{1}=\dots=z_{d}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{q\to 1}(1-q)^{k_{1}+\cdots+k_{d}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{SZ}}(u_{k_{1}}\cdots u_{k_{d}})=\zeta(u_{k_{1}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}):=\sum\limits_{m_{1}>\cdots>m_{d}>0}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots m_{d}^{k_{d}}}.

But in this work, we focus purely on the algebraic structure of (SZ-)
q
MZVs and do not consider its implication for classical Multiple Zeta Values. Over the years, several versions of
q
MZVs were introduced (see, e.g.,
[
10
,
9
,
14
,
4
,
1
]
); for an overview, see
[
5
]
. Because of Conjecture
1
and since the
q
-series on the right of (
0.1
) is invariant under the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear involution
\tau:\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
, defined by
\tau({\mathbf{1}}):={\mathbf{1}}
and

\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}u_{0}^{z_{d}}\right):=u_{z_{d}+1}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{z_{1}+1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}

for all
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}\geq 1,\text{ and }z_{1},\dots,z_{d}\geq 0
(see
[
13
, Theorem 8.3]
), we will consider
the algebra of
formal
q
MZVs
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}:={\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\left(\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},\ast\right)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{T}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\left(\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},\ast\right)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{T}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left(\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},\ast\right)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{T}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\left(\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},\ast\right)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{T}$}}},

where
T
is the
\ast
-ideal in
\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
generated by
\left\{\tau(\mathtt{W})-\mathtt{W}\,|\,\mathtt{W}\in\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\right\}
. For
\mathtt{W}\in\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
, we set
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)
to be the congruence class of
\mathtt{W}
in
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
. Note that depth and weight are invariant under
\tau
while the number of zeros generally is not. Furthermore, playing with
\tau
and the stuffle product
\ast
, one obtains non-trivial
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations among formal
q
MZVs. The following folklore conjecture (see
[
2
]
; a published version can be found in
[
14
, Conjecture 1]
) states the expectation of how the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations among SZ-
q
MZVs look like.

Conjecture 1

(Bachmann)
.

All
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations among elements in
\mathcal{Z}_{q}
are obtained by the stuffle product
\ast
and duality
\tau
.

I.e., one expects
\mathcal{Z}_{q}\simeq\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
. We will consider in this paper only
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations in
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
which are implied by

(1.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast(\mathtt{W}_{2}-\tau(\mathtt{W}_{2}))\right)=0

for any words
\mathtt{W}_{1},\mathtt{W}_{2}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
. For investigating
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
in more detail, we need the following notion of filtrations.

Notation 2
.

(i)

For every
(\mathrm{N},\operatorname{op})\in\{(\mathrm{Z},\operatorname{zero}),(\mathrm{D},\operatorname{depth}),(\mathrm{W},\operatorname{wt})\}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and sets
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},\,\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
, write

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{N}}_{n}\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}\mid\operatorname{op}(\mathtt{W})\leq n\right\}\cap\mathcal{S},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{N}}_{n}\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\mid\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ},\,\operatorname{op}(\mathtt{W})\leq n\right\}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

for the filtration by number of zeros (if
\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{Z}
), depth (if
\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{D}
), and weight (if
\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{W}
) respectively on
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
respectively.

(ii)

For
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
or
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
,
\mathrm{N}_{1},\dots,\mathrm{N}_{m}\in\{\mathrm{Z,D,W}\}
, where
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, and for integers
n_{1},\dots,n_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}
, we abbreviate

(2.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{N}_{1},\dots,\mathrm{N}_{m}}_{n_{1},\dots,n_{m}}\mathcal{S}:=\bigcap\limits_{j=1}^{m}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{N}_{j}}_{n_{j}}\mathcal{S}.

The following particular filtration will play a main role in this paper.

Definition 3
.

We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}:=\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z}}_{0}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

At this point, note that

(3.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\ast\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime}}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z+z^{\prime},d+d^{\prime},w+w^{\prime}}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}

and

(3.2)

\displaystyle\tau\left(\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\right)=\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{w-z-d,d,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}

for all
z,z^{\prime},d,d^{\prime},w,w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
. Hence, considering (
1.1
),
\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2}
and
\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\tau(\mathtt{W}_{2})
are, in general, in different filtrations of
\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
regarding the number of zeros since we have, in general
z\neq w-z-d
. Therefore, for given
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
, it is difficult to find the minimal
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z}}_{z}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
.

Let us consider a small example of how we use
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations of shape (
1.1
) to obtain that, e.g.,
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
for
\mathtt{W}=u_{2}u_{0}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
. First, we note that

\displaystyle u_{2}u_{0}=u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{0}-2u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}-u_{1}u_{1}-u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}.

Now,

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\,\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast(u_{1}u_{0}-\tau(u_{1}u_{0}))\right)-2\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left({\mathbf{1}}\ast(u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}-\tau(u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}))\right)

\displaystyle\,-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left({\mathbf{1}}\ast(u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}-\tau(u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}))\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{0}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{2}\right)-2\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}\right)+2\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\,-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{2}\right),

and so,

(3.3)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}\right)=\,

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{2}\right)-2\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{2}\right)

(3.4)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{2}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{3}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{2}\right)\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}.

That formal
q
MZVs are in
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
already is not just a coincidence, as the following conjecture shows.

Conjecture 4

(Bachmann,
[
4
, Conjecture 3.9]
)
.

For all
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

(4.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}.

In particular, we have
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}=\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
.

We say that Bachmann’s Conjecture
4

is true for

(z_{0},d_{0},w_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
if (
4.1
) is true for
(z,d,w)=(z_{0},d_{0},w_{0})
.

Partial results already exist; we will collect them in the following.

Theorem 5
.

(i)

By Bachmann (
[
4
, Proposition 4.4]
), Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(z,1,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

(ii)

also by Bachmann (
[
4
, Proposition 5.9]
), Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(1,2,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

(iii)

by Vleeshouwers (
[
12
, Theorem 5.3]
), Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(z,2,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with some parity condition on
w
,

(iv)

and by Burmester (
[
6
, Theorem 6.4]
), Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(1,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

While the proofs of (i)–(iii) are mainly based on generating series of the corresponding
q
-series, the proof of (iv) uses the stuffle product and duality relations. Using relations among formal Multiple Zeta Values of shape (
1.1
) only suffices to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 6

(Theorem
26
)
.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
, and consider integers
1\leq j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq d
. Deconcatenate
\mathbf{k}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1})}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{j_{1}}),\ \mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}=(k_{j_{1}+1},\dots,k_{j_{2}}),\ \mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}=(k_{j_{2}+1},\dots,k_{d}).

We have

(6.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1})}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

where
w=|\mathbf{k}|+z
.

Remark 7
.

(i)

Theorem
6
is a generalization of Bachmann’s Theorem
[
4
, Proposition 4.4]
via the case
d=1
. We have already seen the proof for an example of this theorem using our methods in (
3.3
). We will generalize this approach in Proposition
21
to generalize Bachmann’s Theorem
5
(i).

(ii)

Note that Theorem
6
also generalizes Burmester’s Theorem
[
6
, Theorem 6.4]
via considering the special cases
z=1
. For details, we refer to Corollary
28
.

Extending our methods of playing with relations of shape (
1.1
), we observe the following theorem.

Theorem 8

(Theorem
80
)
.

Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(z,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with
z+d\leq 6
.

In this paper, we will use duality and the stuffle product only for an approach to write
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)
for every
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
satisfying
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})\geq 1
as linear combination of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}^{\prime}\right)
’s with
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W}^{\prime})<\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})
and
\mathtt{W}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
. We need the following notion of
\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
for this.

Definition 9
.

For
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we define

\displaystyle\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}:=\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w-1}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}z^{\prime}+d^{\prime}=z+d-1\\
0\leq z^{\prime}\leq z\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

In this paper, our main approach towards Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is to strengthen the conjecture as follows and then to investigate the strengthened version for obtaining results like Theorem
8
.

Conjecture 10

(Refined Bachmann Conjecture)
.

For all
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

(10.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}.

We say that the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10

is true for

(z_{0},d_{0},w_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
, if (
10.1
) is true for
(z,d,w)=(z_{0},d_{0},w_{0})
.

Lemma 11

(Lemma
73
)
.

Fix
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. If the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for
(z,d,w)
and if Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with
z^{\prime}+d^{\prime}+w^{\prime}<z+d+w
, then Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for
(z,d,w)
. In particular, the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
implies Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
.

To study the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
, we will introduce the box product (see Definition
15
) that provides a connection to the stuffle product (see Lemma
61
) and allows us to refine the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
for
z\geq d
again (see Conjecture
39
). In this way, we obtain another particular result towards the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
.

Theorem 12

(Theorem
81
)
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all triples of positive integers
(z,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{>0}
with
1\leq d\leq 4
.

Theorem
12
will follow mainly using Theorem
8
and the investigation of the box product from Section
4
. Furthermore, Theorem
12
is a strong statement since - together with some more results of this paper - now, Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is almost proven for
z+d\leq 7
as well: Namely, following Lemma
11
, it remains to prove the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
for triples of shape
(2,5,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

All our main results (and those implied by the box product) are based on
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations of shape (
1.1
) only. Following our approach to a general proof of the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
(and so of Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
too), described in Section
5
, it is conjecturally possible to prove the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
using
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations of shape (
1.1
) only. Based on our results, it seems that this approach works. Furthermore, our explicit approach has the advantage that it is (compared to other approaches) easy to obtain explicit formulas for
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)
(with
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
) as element of
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
. Proposition
21
, for example, contains such an explicit formula. Nevertheless, the explicitness limits this method in the sense that the larger
z+d
is in the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
, the more confusing the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear relations (
1.1
), one needs to consider following our approach, become.

Organization of the paper.

Section
2
contains the introduction of the box product mentioned. Section
3
contains generalizations of theorems by Bachmann and Burmester concerning the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
, like Theorem
6
. In Section
4
, we will investigate the
box product
and consider its connection to the stuffle product. Furthermore, Section
5
contains the rough description of our approach to the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
. Using the approach from Section
5
, in Section
6
, we prove new partial results towards Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
. Particularly, there, we will provide proofs for Theorems
8
and
12
. Last, Section
7
ends the paper with some open questions and a rough generalization of our calculations from Section
6
.

Acknowledgements.

The author thanks Henrik Bachmann, Annika Burmester, Jan-Willem van Ittersum, and Ulf Kühn for valuable discussions and helpful comments on this paper.

2.
Introduction of the box product

In this section, we introduce the box product and consider elementary properties. First, we briefly remark on a property of the stuffle product in the following proposition.

Proposition 13
.

Let be
\mathtt{W}_{1},\mathtt{W}_{2}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
and write

\displaystyle z=\operatorname{zero}(\tau(\mathtt{W}_{1}))+\operatorname{zero}(\tau(\mathtt{W}_{2})),\ d_{1}=\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W}_{1}),\ d_{2}=\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W}_{2}),\ w=\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{1})+\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{2}).

Then, for
0\leq s\leq\min\{d_{1},d_{2}\}
, there are uniquely determined

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}+s}\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}\,|\,\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W})=\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}+s\right\}

such that

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2}=\sum\limits_{s=0}^{\min\{d_{1},d_{2}\}}\mathcal{L}_{\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}+s}.

Furthermore, for all
0\leq s\leq\min\{d_{1},d_{2}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\tau\left(\mathcal{L}_{\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}+s}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}+s,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}.

In particular,
\tau\left(\mathcal{L}_{\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}}\right)
is the part of
\tau(\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2})
having the maximum number of zeros and we have

\displaystyle\tau(\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2})\in\sum\limits_{s=0}^{\min\{d_{1},d_{2}\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}+s,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Equations (
3.1
) and (
3.2
).
∎

Let us consider an example to point out the statement of Proposition
13
.

Example 14
.

Choose
\mathtt{W}_{1}=u_{2},\,\mathtt{W}_{2}=u_{1}u_{2}
, i.e.,
d_{1}=1,\,d_{2}=2
in the notion of Proposition
13
. We have

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2}=

\displaystyle\,\underbrace{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1953,0}u_{3}u_{2}+u_{1}u_{4}}}_{=\,\mathcal{L}_{2}}+\underbrace{u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}+2u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}}_{=\,\mathcal{L}_{3}}.

Observe

\displaystyle\tau(\mathcal{L}_{2})=\,{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1953,0}u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}u_{0}u_{0}+u_{1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}u_{1}},\quad\tau(\mathcal{L}_{3})=\,u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}+2u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}.

We see that
\tau(\mathcal{L}_{2})
indeed has the maximum number of zeros in the expression
\tau(u_{2}\ast u_{1}u_{2})
.

Since we want to reduce the number of zeros, we often will be interested in the part of the stuffle product only that has the maximum number of zeros. Therefore, Proposition
13
motivates the definition of the
box product
that basically extracts this part of the stuffle product after one applies
\tau
.

Definition 15

(Box product)
.

The
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear
box product

\boxast\colon\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\times\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}\to\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
is defined as follows: For
\mathtt{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
with
\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W}_{j})=d_{j}
, where
j\in\{1,2\}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}_{1}\boxast\mathtt{W}_{2}:=\mathcal{L}_{\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}}

in the notion of Proposition
13
.

For illustration, we continue Example
14
.

Example 16
.

We have

\displaystyle u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}=u_{3}u_{2}+u_{1}u_{4},

which is exactly
\mathcal{L}_{2}
of Example
14
, i.e., after applying
\tau
, one obtains the part of the stuffle product
u_{2}\ast u_{1}u_{2}
having maximum number of zeros. We state and prove the generalization of this observation in Lemma
61
.

Corollary 17
.

Let be
\mathtt{W}_{1},\mathtt{W}_{2}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
and write

\displaystyle z=\operatorname{zero}(\tau(\mathtt{W}_{1}))+\operatorname{zero}(\tau(\mathtt{W}_{2})),\ d_{1}=\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W}_{1}),\ d_{2}=\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W}_{2}),\ w=\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{1})+\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{2}).

Then,

\displaystyle\tau(\mathtt{W}_{1}\ast\mathtt{W}_{2})-\tau(\mathtt{W}_{1}\boxast\mathtt{W}_{2})\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{\min\{d_{1},d_{2}\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}+s,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Proposition
13
and the definition of the box product.
∎

Lemma 18
.

Consider the alphabet
\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}=\{u_{j}\mid j\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\}
. The restriction of the box product
\boxast\colon\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\rangle\times\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\rangle\to\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\rangle
can be described as follows. For any two words
\mathtt{W}_{1}=u_{n_{1}}\cdots u_{n_{s}},

\mathtt{W}_{2}=u_{\ell_{1}}\cdots u_{\ell_{r}}\in\left(\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\right)^{\ast}
, we set recursively

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}_{1}\tilde{\boxast}\mathtt{W}_{2}:=\begin{cases}0,\ \text{if }s>r,\\
\mathtt{W}_{2},\,\text{if }\mathtt{W}_{1}={\mathbf{1}},\\
u_{\ell_{1}}\left(\mathtt{W}_{1}\tilde{\boxast}u_{\ell_{2}}\cdots u_{\ell_{r}}\right)+u_{n_{1}+\ell_{1}}\left(u_{n_{2}}\cdots u_{n_{s}}\tilde{\boxast}u_{\ell_{2}}\cdots u_{\ell_{r}}\right),\ \text{if }s\leq r.\end{cases}

Then,
\mathtt{W}_{1}\tilde{\boxast}\mathtt{W}_{2}=\mathtt{W}_{1}\boxast\mathtt{W}_{2}
whenever
\operatorname{len}(\mathtt{W}_{1})\leq\operatorname{len}(\mathtt{W}_{2})
.

Note that the box product satisfies the following connection to the stuffle product.

Lemma 19
.

For all indices of positive integers
\boldsymbol{n_{1}},\boldsymbol{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{\ell}
, we have

\displaystyle u_{\boldsymbol{n_{1}}}\boxast(u_{\boldsymbol{n_{2}}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})=(u_{\boldsymbol{n_{1}}}\ast u_{\boldsymbol{n_{2}}})\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=u_{\boldsymbol{n_{2}}}\boxast(u_{\boldsymbol{n_{1}}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}).

Proof.

The proof of the first equality follows by induction on
\operatorname{len}(\boldsymbol{n_{1}})+\operatorname{len}(\boldsymbol{n_{2}})
and the definition of stuffle and box product. The second equality then follows from the commutativity of the stuffle product and the first equality.
∎

Next, we make an easy but instrumental observation. For this, we denote for an given index
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{r})
its
reversed index
by
\operatorname{rev}(\mathbf{k}):=(k_{r},\dots,k_{1})
.

Proposition 20
.

Given
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{s},\,\boldsymbol{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
1\leq s\leq d
. Writing

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=\sum\limits_{\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}}a_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}

with
a_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\mathbb{Z}
appropriate, we have

\displaystyle u_{\operatorname{rev}(\mathbf{n})}\boxast u_{\operatorname{rev}(\boldsymbol{\ell})}=\sum\limits_{\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}}a_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}u_{\operatorname{rev}(\boldsymbol{\mu})}.

Proof.

Using Lemma
18
and induction on
\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{n})+\operatorname{len}(\boldsymbol{\ell})
, the claim follows immediately.
∎

3.
A common approach to theorems by Bachmann and Burmester

In this section, we consider the cases of
d=1
(and
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
arbitrary), and
z=1
(and
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
arbitrary), respectively, of Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
. The first case mainly is a result originally due to Bachmann (
[
4
, Proposition 4.4]
), which we will reprove in a way giving explicit formulas for every element of
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
as linear combination of elements in
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
. The second case is done by Burmester’s thesis (
[
6
, Theorem 6.4]
), which we will extend in Section
3.2
.

3.1.
Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
for
(z,1,w)

By
[
4
, Proposition 4.4]
(see also Theorem
5
(i)), it is known that Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all triples
(z,1,w)
. Here, we give an alternative proof which gives an explicit expression in terms of elements in
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
.

Proposition 21
.

For all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have that
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k}u_{0}^{z}\right)
equals

(21.1)

\displaystyle(-1)^{z}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\geq 0\\
j_{1}+j_{2}=z\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{0},\dots,n_{j_{2}}\geq 0\\
n_{0}+\cdots+n_{j_{2}}=k-1\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq p\leq j_{2}\\
0\leq\varepsilon_{p}\leq\min\{1,n_{p}\}\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{n_{j_{2}}-\varepsilon_{j_{2}}+1}\cdots u_{n_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}+1}u_{n_{0}+1}u_{1}^{j_{1}}\right)

(21.2)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{1\leq j\leq z}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{j}\geq 1\\
\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{j}\leq z\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\geq 0\\
j_{1}+j_{2}=z-\ell_{1}-\cdots-\ell_{j}\end{subarray}}

(21.3)

\displaystyle\,\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{0},\dots,n_{j_{2}}\geq 0\\
n_{0}+\cdots+n_{j_{2}}=k-1\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq p\leq j_{2}\\
0\leq\varepsilon_{p}\leq\min\{1,n_{p}\}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{z-j}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}^{\ell_{1}}\ast\cdots\ast u_{1}^{\ell_{j}}\ast u_{n_{j_{2}}-\varepsilon_{j_{2}}+1}\cdots u_{n_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}+1}u_{n_{0}+1}u_{1}^{j_{1}}\right).

In particular, we have
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k}u_{0}^{z}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+1,k+z}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
, yielding Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
for all triples
(z,1,w)
.

Proof.

First note that a calculation, using the definition of the stuffle product, shows for all
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
the identity

(21.4)

\displaystyle u_{a}u_{0}^{b}=\,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\ell=1}^{a-1}(-1)^{\ell-1}u_{1}^{\ell}\ast u_{a-\ell}u_{0}^{b}+(-1)^{a-1}h(a,b),

where
h(a,b):=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\geq 0\\
j_{1}+j_{2}=a-1\end{subarray}}u_{1}^{j_{1}+1}\left(u_{1}^{j_{2}}\ast u_{0}^{b}\right)
. Choosing
a=z+1
and
b=k-1
, we obtain

(21.5)

\displaystyle u_{z+1}u_{0}^{k-1}=\,\sum\limits_{\ell=1}^{z}(-1)^{\ell-1}u_{1}^{\ell}\ast u_{z+1-\ell}u_{0}^{k-1}+(-1)^{z}h(z+1,k-1).

Using the latter formula and (
21.4
) repeatedly, we obtain

(21.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{z+1}u_{0}^{k-1}=&\,\sum\limits_{1\leq j\leq z}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{j}\geq 1\\
\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{j}\leq z\end{subarray}}(-1)^{z-j}u_{1}^{\ell_{1}}\ast\cdots\ast u_{1}^{\ell_{j}}\ast h(z+1-\ell_{1}-\cdots-\ell_{j},k-1)\\
&\,+(-1)^{z}h(z+1,k-1).\end{split}

Now, note that for all
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

\displaystyle h(a,b)=

\displaystyle\,\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\geq 0\\
j_{1}+j_{2}=a-1\end{subarray}}u_{1}^{j_{1}+1}\left(u_{1}^{j_{2}}\ast u_{0}^{b}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\geq 0\\
j_{1}+j_{2}=a-1\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{0},\dots,n_{j_{2}}\geq 0\\
n_{0}+\cdots+n_{j_{2}}=b\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq p\leq j_{2}\\
0\leq\varepsilon_{p}\leq\min\{1,n_{p}\}\end{subarray}}u_{1}^{j_{1}+1}u_{0}^{n_{0}}u_{1}u_{0}^{n_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}}\cdots u_{1}u_{0}^{n_{j_{2}}-\varepsilon_{j_{2}}}.

Hence, by
\tau
-invariance of formal
q
MZVs,

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(h(a,b)\right)=

\displaystyle\,\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\geq 0\\
j_{1}+j_{2}=a-1\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{0},\dots,n_{j_{2}}\geq 0\\
n_{0}+\cdots+n_{j_{2}}=b\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq p\leq j_{2}\\
0\leq\varepsilon_{p}\leq\min\{1,n_{p}\}\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{n_{j_{2}}-\varepsilon_{j_{2}}+1}\cdots u_{n_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}+1}u_{n_{0}+1}u_{1}^{j_{1}}\right),

implying the claim when using (
21.6
) and
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k}u_{0}^{z}\right)=\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{k}u_{0}^{z})\right)=\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z+1}u_{0}^{k-1}\right)
. From the obtained representation of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k}u_{0}^{z}\right)
, we get directly
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k}u_{0}^{z}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+1,k+z}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
due to (
3.1
).
∎

Let us consider an example regarding Proposition
21
.

Example 22
.

For
k=z=2
, Proposition
21
yields

\displaystyle\,\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{1}\ast u_{2}\right)-2\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{2}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{2}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}\ast u_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\,+3\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}\right)+2\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}\right)+3\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{4}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{3}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{2}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{2}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{2,4}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{3,4}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}.

3.2.
Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
for
(1,d,w)

Given an index
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
, we introduce the following notation of subindices

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1};j_{2})}:=\begin{cases}(k_{j_{1}},\dots,k_{j_{2}}),\quad&\text{if }1\leq j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq d,\\
\emptyset,\quad&\text{else.}\end{cases}

Lemma 23
.

Fix
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
. For
1\leq j\leq d
, we have

(23.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(2;j)}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

where
w=|\mathbf{k}|+z
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
d
. The base case
d=1
corresponds to Proposition
21
since then
j=1
and so
\mathbf{k}_{(2;j)}=\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}=\emptyset
. Hence, we may assume
d>1
and that Lemma
23
is proven already for all smaller values of
d
. First, note that the case
j=d
follows from
\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}=\emptyset
in this case and from

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\dots,n_{s^{\prime}}\geq 1\\
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{s^{\prime}}=z\\
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq d\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{n_{1}}\cdots u_{n_{s^{\prime}}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}}\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}

since

(23.2)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\dots,n_{s^{\prime}}\geq 1\\
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{s^{\prime}}=z\\
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq d\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{n_{1}}\cdots u_{n_{s^{\prime}}}\ast\tau(u_{\mathbf{k}})\right)

(23.3)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\dots,n_{s^{\prime}}\geq 1\\
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{s^{\prime}}=z\\
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq d\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{n_{1}}\cdots u_{n_{s^{\prime}}}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

(23.4)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\dots,n_{s^{\prime}}\geq 1\\
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{s^{\prime}}=z\\
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq d\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau\left(u_{n_{1}}\cdots u_{n_{s^{\prime}}}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\right)

(23.5)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\dots,n_{s^{\prime}}\geq 1\\
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{s^{\prime}}=z\\
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq d\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau\left(u_{n_{1}}\cdots u_{n_{s^{\prime}}}\boxast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\right)\mod\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

The last identity is a consequence of Proposition
13
and the definition of the box product. Furthermore, the remaining expression is

\displaystyle\equiv\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(2;d)}}\ast u_{0}^{z}\right)\right)\mod\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

which can be verified via induction on
s^{\prime}+d
and the definition of the stuffle product. Hence, let be
1\leq j\leq d-1
and assume that the claim holds for all larger values of
j
. The induction hypothesis on
d
implies, since
\operatorname{len}(\emptyset)+\operatorname{len}\left(\mathbf{k}_{(j+2;d)}\right)=d-j-1<d-1
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)=\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{j+1}}(u_{\emptyset}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+2;d))}}u_{0}^{z})\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d-j+s,w^{\prime}},

where
w^{\prime}=|\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}|+z
. Hence, by (
3.1
), we obtain

(23.6)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j)}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

Now, using the definition of the stuffle product, we obtain

(23.7)

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j)}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}=

\displaystyle\,u_{k_{1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(2;j)}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)+u_{k_{j+1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j)}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+2;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)

(23.8)

\displaystyle\,+u_{k_{1}+k_{j+1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(2;j)}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+2;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right).

Note that the formal
q
MZVof the second summand on the right-hand side is an element of
\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
due to the assumption on
j
, while the formal
q
MZV of the third one is by induction hypothesis on
d
. Hence, because of (
23.6
), we obtain

(23.9)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(2;j)}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

completing the induction step. Therefore, the lemma is proven.
∎

Corollary 24
.

Fix
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. For all
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
, we have

(24.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}}u_{0}^{z}\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

where
w=|\mathbf{k}|+z
.

Proof.

This is the special case
j=1
of Lemma
23
.
∎

Corollary 25
.

Fix
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. For all
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
, we have

(25.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}}u_{0}u_{0}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+2,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ},

where
w=|\mathbf{k}|+2
.

Proof.

The special case
z=2
of Corollary
24
and
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{1,d+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+2,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
by Burmester’s Theorem
5
(iv) yield the claim.
∎

Lemma
23
is a special case of the following theorem.

Theorem 26

(Theorem
6
)
.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
, and consider integers
1\leq j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq d
. We have

(26.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1})}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

where
w=|\mathbf{k}|+z
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
d
. Note that the base case
d=1
follows from Proposition
21
since then
j_{1}=j_{2}=1
and so
\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}=\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}=\emptyset
. Hence, choose
d>1
and assume the theorem is proven for all smaller values of
d
. Furthermore, note that the case
j_{1}=1
is nothing else than Lemma
23
. Hence, let
2\leq j_{1}\leq d
arbitrary. The claim for
j_{2}=j_{1}
corresponds to Corollary
24
since then
\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}=\emptyset
. Therefore, assume
j_{2}>j_{1}>1
in the following and that the claim is proven for all possible smaller values of
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
and
\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})})=j_{2}-j_{1}
, respectively. Using the recursive definition of the stuffle product gives

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1})}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1}-1)}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}}\ast u_{k_{j_{1}}}u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)

\displaystyle\,-u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1}-1)}}u_{k_{j_{1}+1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+2;j_{2})}}\ast u_{k_{j_{1}}}u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)

\displaystyle\,-u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1}-1)}}u_{k_{j_{1}}+k_{j_{1}+1}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+2;j_{2})}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)

Now, the formal
q
MZVof the first summand on the right-hand side is in
\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
due to the assumption on
j_{1}
(since
\operatorname{len}\left(\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1}-1)}\right)=\operatorname{len}\left(\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1})}\right)-1
), while the second one is as well due to the assumption on
j_{2}-j_{1}
(since
\operatorname{len}\left(\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+2;j_{2})}\right)=\operatorname{len}\left(\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}\right)-1
), and the third one is due to the induction hypothesis on
d
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j_{1})}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{1}+1;j_{2})}}\ast u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}}u_{0}^{z}\right)\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

completing the induction step. Hence, the theorem follows.
∎

Corollary 27
.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
. For all
1\leq j\leq d
, we have

(27.1)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{j},\dots,\ell_{d}\geq 0\\
\ell_{j}+\cdots+\ell_{d}=z\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j-1)}}u_{k_{j}}u_{0}^{\ell_{j}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}u_{0}^{\ell_{d}}\right)\in\sum\limits_{s=1}^{z}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

where
w=|\mathbf{k}|+z
.

Proof.

For fixed
1\leq j\leq d
, the corollary is obtained from the special case
j_{1}=j
,
j_{2}=d
of Theorem
26
and multiplying out the corresponding stuffle product occurring in (
26.1
) (since then
\mathbf{k}_{(j_{2}+1;d)}=\emptyset
).
∎

As a corollary of Corollary
27
, we obtain Burmester’s Theorem
5
(iv).

Corollary 28

(Burmester,
[
6
, Theorem 6.4]
)
.

Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(1,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

Proof.

Let be
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
and denote
w=|\mathbf{k}|+1
in the following. Considering Corollary
27
with
z=1
and
j=d
, we obtain
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}}u_{0}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
. Now, let be
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq d-1
. Considering the difference of (
27.1
) with
z=1
,
j=j^{\prime}
and (
27.1
) with
z=1
,
j=j^{\prime}+1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{k}_{(1;j^{\prime})}}u_{0}u_{\mathbf{k}_{(j^{\prime}+1;d)}}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}.

In particular, for every
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}\cap\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{1,d,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
, we have shown
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
, i.e., we have
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{1,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
, completing the claim.
∎

Corollary 29
.

Let be
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
. We have

(29.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{\mathbf{k}_{(3;d)}}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+2,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ},

where
w=|\mathbf{k}|+2
.

Proof.

Consider the difference of (
27.1
) for
z=2
,
j=2
, and (
27.1
) for
z=2
,
j=3
to obtain, all congruences modulo
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{1,d+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}
,

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{3},\dots,\ell_{d}\geq 0\\
\ell_{3}+\cdots+\ell_{d}=2\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{\ell_{3}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}u_{0}^{\ell_{d}}\right)-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{d}\geq 0\\
\ell_{2}+\cdots+\ell_{d}=2\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{\ell_{2}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}u_{0}^{\ell_{d}}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{\mathbf{k}_{(3;d)}}\right)-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{3},\dots,\ell_{d}\geq 0\\
\ell_{3}+\cdots+\ell_{d}=1\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{\ell_{3}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}u_{0}^{\ell_{d}}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{3}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{3},\dots,\ell_{d}\geq 0\\
\ell_{3}+\cdots+\ell_{d}=1\end{subarray}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\ell_{d}+1}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{\ell_{3}+1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{\mathbf{k}_{(3;d)}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{\mathbf{k}_{(3;d)}}\right).

Since
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{1,d+1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{d+2,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
by Corollary
28
, the claim follows.
∎

4.
Investigation of the box product

First, in Section
4.1
, we show that several monomials can already be written as a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of non-trivial box products. In Section
4.2
, we investigate a conjecture (Conjecture
39
) regarding the structure of box products and give partial results for it. Furthermore, in Section
4.4
, we study the main connection between the box product and the stuffle product that we will need to prove our main results. Last, in Section
4.5
, we give some further results about the box product that are interesting for itself but not necessary for the remaining paper.

4.1.
Monomials as linear combination of box products

In the following, we characterize some particular monomials in
\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\rangle
as a linear combination of box products. The results will be important for proving Theorem
81
.

We will need the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space spanned by (non-trivial) box products in the following.

Definition 30
.

We define

(30.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{\mathtt{W}_{1}\boxast\mathtt{W}_{2}\mid\mathtt{W}_{1},\,\mathtt{W}_{2}\in\left(\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\right)^{\ast},\,\mathtt{W}_{1},\mathtt{W}_{2}\neq{\mathbf{1}}\right\}\subset\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\rangle.

Corollary 31
.

Given
\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Then
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\mathcal{P}
if and only if
u_{\operatorname{rev}(\boldsymbol{\mu})}\in\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Proposition
20
.
∎

Lemma 32
.

For all
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
0\leq j\leq d-1
, we have

\displaystyle u_{2}^{d},\ u_{1}^{j}u_{1+d}u_{1}^{d-j-1}\in\mathcal{P}.

Proof.

A direct calculation shows
u_{2}^{d}=\,u_{1}^{d}\boxast u_{1}^{d}
, giving the first part of the lemma. Furthermore, for all
0\leq j\leq d-1
, we have

\displaystyle u_{1}^{j}u_{1+d}u_{1}^{d-j-1}=\,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{a=1}^{d}(-1)^{a-1}\,u_{1}^{a}\boxast u_{1}^{j}u_{1+d-a}u_{1}^{d-j-1},

giving the second claim of the lemma. ∎

Lemma 33
.

For arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
0\leq j\leq d-2
, we have

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-2}\in\mathcal{P}.

Proof.

For any
0\leq j\leq d-2
, one verifies

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-2}

∎

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{a=1}^{j+1}(-1)^{a+1}\,u_{1}^{j-a+1}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j-2}\boxast u_{a}u_{1}^{d-1}+\sum\limits_{a=1}^{j+2}(-1)^{a+1}\,u_{1}^{d-a+1}\boxast u_{a}u_{1}^{d-1}.

We first need an auxiliary lemma to prove the statements in Corollary
35
and Lemma
36
.

Lemma 34
.

For all
d,\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}\leq d+2
, we have

\displaystyle u_{\mu_{1}}u_{\mu_{2}}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}+2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})\in\mathcal{P}.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
\mu_{1}
. First, consider
\mu_{1}=1
. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
33
, we obtain by direct calculation that

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{\mu_{2}}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{2}+1}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-u_{1}u_{\mu_{2}-1}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{2}+2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq a\leq\mu_{2}-3\\
0\leq b\leq 1+a\end{subarray}}(-1)^{b}\,u_{1}^{d-\mu_{2}+b+2}\boxast u_{2+a-b}u_{\mu_{2}-2-a}u_{1}^{d-2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq a\leq\mu_{2}-3\\
0\leq b\leq a\end{subarray}}(-1)^{a+b}\,u_{1}^{a-b}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-\mu_{2}+1}\boxast u_{1+b}u_{\mu_{2}-2-a}u_{1}^{d-2}.

Hence, we have for all
\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
that
u_{1}u_{\mu_{2}}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{2}+1}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})\in\mathcal{P}
if and only if we have
u_{1}u_{\mu_{2}-1}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{2}+2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})\in\mathcal{P}
, giving recursively that
u_{1}u_{\mu_{2}}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{2}+1}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})\in\mathcal{P}
if and only if

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{3}(u_{1}^{d-2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})\in\mathcal{P},

which is true since this is the
j=0
case of Lemma
33
.

Now, for
\mu_{1}>1
, assume that the lemma is proven for
\mu_{1}-1
already. We calculate

\displaystyle u_{\mu_{1}}u_{\mu_{2}}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}+2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{a=\mu_{2}}^{d-\mu_{1}+2}(-1)^{\mu_{2}+a}\,u_{1}^{d-\ell_{1}-a+3}\boxast u_{\mu_{1}-1}u_{a}u_{1}^{d-2}

\displaystyle\quad-u_{\mu_{1}-1}u_{\ell_{2}}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}+3}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{d-\mu_{1}+1-\mu_{2}}\,u_{\mu_{1}-1}u_{d-\mu_{1}+3}u_{1}^{d-2}.

I.e., we have
u_{\mu_{1}}u_{\mu_{2}}(u_{1}^{d-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}+2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})\in\mathcal{P}
by the assumption that the lemma is proven for
\mu_{1}-1
.
∎

Corollary 35
.

For all
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
0\leq j\leq d
, we have

\displaystyle u_{1+j}u_{d-j+1}u_{1}^{d-2}\in\mathcal{P}.

Proof.

Setting
\mu_{1}=1+j
and
\mu_{2}=d-j+1
in Lemma
34
, we obtain the claim.
∎

Furthermore, Lemma
34
is used to prove the following observation.

Lemma 36
.

For arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and all
0\leq j\leq d-3
, we have

\displaystyle u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-3}\in\mathcal{P}.

Proof.

First, a direct calculation gives for all
0\leq j\leq d-3
that

\displaystyle u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-3}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{a=2}^{j+2}(-1)^{a}\,u_{1}^{j-a+2}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j-3}\boxast u_{a}u_{1}^{d-1}+\sum\limits_{a=1}^{j+3}(-1)^{a}\,u_{1}^{d-a+1}\boxast u_{a}u_{1}^{d-1}

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\geq 2\\
a+b\leq j+3\end{subarray}}(-1)^{a+b}\,u_{1}^{j-(a+b)+3}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j-3}\boxast u_{a}u_{b}u_{1}^{d-2}

\displaystyle+(-1)^{j+3}\,u_{2}u_{j+3}(u_{1}^{d-j-3}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{j+3}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\geq 2\\
a+b=j+3\end{subarray}}u_{a+2}u_{b+1}(u_{1}^{d-j-4}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2})+u_{a+2}u_{b}(u_{1}^{d-j-3}\boxast u_{1}^{d-2}).

Using Lemma
34
now yields the claim.
∎

Collecting the results of this subsection, we have proven the following theorem.

Theorem 37
.

Let be
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

(i)

For all
0\leq j\leq d-2
, we have

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-2},\ u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-2}u_{1}\in\mathcal{P}.

(ii)

For all
0\leq j\leq d
, we have

\displaystyle u_{1+j}u_{d-j+1}u_{1}^{d-2},\ u_{1}^{d-2}u_{d-j+1}u_{1+j}\in\mathcal{P}.

(iii)

For all
0\leq j\leq d-3
, we have

\displaystyle u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-3},\ u_{2}^{j}u_{3}u_{2}^{d-j-3}u_{1}u_{2}\in\mathcal{P}.

Proof.

Using Corollary
31
each, the proof for (i) follows from Lemma
33
, the proof of (ii) follows from Corollary
35
, and the proof of (iii) follows from Lemma
36
.
∎

4.2.
Conjectures about particular box products and implications

We consider in this section the structure of all box products
u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
such that
\operatorname{len}(\boldsymbol{\ell})
and
|\mathbf{n}|+|\boldsymbol{\ell}|
are fixed. For this, we will need the spaces
{S}_{z,d}
and
{T}_{z,d}
in the following.

Definition 38
.

(i)

For all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we define

\displaystyle{T}_{z,d}:=

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\mid\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d},\,|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d\right\},

\displaystyle{t}_{z,d}:=

\displaystyle\,\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}{T}_{z,d}.

(ii)

Furthermore, for all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{z,d}:=

\displaystyle\,\left\{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\,\Big|\,\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{s},\,\boldsymbol{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d},\,1\leq s\leq d,\,|\mathbf{n}|+|\boldsymbol{\ell}|=z+d\right\},

\displaystyle{j}_{z,d,:}=

\displaystyle\,\#\mathcal{J}_{z,d}.

and

\displaystyle{S}_{z,d}:=

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\mid(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}\right\}={T}_{z,d}\cap\mathcal{P},

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d}:=

\displaystyle\,\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}{S}_{z,d}.

Based on numerical calculations (see Lemma
42
), we conjecture the following for the dimension of
{S}_{z,d}
.

Conjecture 39
.

For all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

(39.1)

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d}=\binom{z+d-1}{\min\{z,d\}-1}.

Given
(z_{0},d_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we say that Conjecture
39

is true for

(z_{0},d_{0})
if (
39.1
) is true for
(z,d)=(z_{0},d_{0})
. Note the following equivalent formulation for
z\geq d
.

Corollary 40
.

Given
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{>0}
with
z\geq d
. Conjecture
39
is true for
(z,d)
if and only if
{S}_{z,d}={T}_{z,d}
.

Proof.

Clearly, for all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, one has

\displaystyle{t}_{z,d}=\binom{z+d-1}{d-1}

since
{t}_{z,d}
is the number of compositions of
z+d
into exactly
d
positive integers. Hence, for
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{>0}
with
z\geq d
, Conjecture
39
is equivalent to
{s}_{z,d}={t}_{z,d}
which is equivalent to
{S}_{z,d}={T}_{z,d}
since
{S}_{z,d}\subset{T}_{z,d}
and both
{S}_{z,d}
and
{T}_{z,d}
are finite-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces.
∎

Theorem 41
.

Fix
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. If Conjecture
39
is true for
(d,d)
, then it is also true for all
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
with
z>d
.

Proof.

Fix
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and assume that Conjecture
39
is true for
(d,d)
. I.e., by Corollary
40
, we assume
{S}_{d,d}={T}_{d,d}
. This is equivalent to

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{z}}=\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{d,d}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\mathbf{z})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}

for all
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{z}|=2d
and with
a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\mathbf{z})\in\mathbb{Q}
appropriate.

Now, assume
z>d
and let be
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{z}|=z+d
arbitrary. We can write

\displaystyle(z_{1},\dots,z_{d})=(z_{1}^{\prime}+\delta_{1},\dots,z_{d}^{\prime}+\delta_{d})

with
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\mathbf{z^{\prime}}=(z_{1}^{\prime},\dots,z_{d}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{z^{\prime}}|=2d
. Hence,

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{z}}=\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{d,d}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\mathbf{z^{\prime}})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\ell_{1}+\delta_{1}}\cdots u_{\ell_{d}+\delta_{d}}.

Since
\mathbf{z}
was chosen arbitrary, we obtain
{S}_{z,d}={T}_{z,d}
, proving the theorem.
∎

Lemma 42
.

Conjecture
39
is true for all
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
with
1\leq d\leq 8
.

Proof.

The proof for
1\leq z\leq d\leq 8
is obtained by computer algebra; for details, see Remark
65
and the appendix. By Theorem
41
, Conjecture
39
is also true for
z\geq d
when
1\leq d\leq 8
, proving the lemma.
∎

Note that
{s}_{z,d}
is the dimension of the image of the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}\colon\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{J}_{z,d}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{T}_{z,d},

\displaystyle(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})

\displaystyle\longmapsto u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}

that we continue
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinearly. By the rank-nullity theorem, we know that

(42.1)

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d}+\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{J}_{z,d}.

The right-hand side is given by
{j}_{z,d}
, which is the number of writing
z+d
as ordered sum of at least
d+1
and at most
d+\min\{z,d\}
positive integers, i.e.,

(42.2)

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{J}_{z,d}={j}_{z,d}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\min\{z,d\}}\binom{z+d-1}{d+j-1}.

Hence, determining
{s}_{z,d}
now is equivalent to determining
\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}
. While it seems to be difficult to obtain a (conjectured) basis of
{S}_{z,d}
, we can give a conjectured basis of
\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}
explicitly. To do so, we need the notion of stuffle product and box product on index level. I.e., we set
\mathbf{n}\ast\emptyset:=\emptyset\ast\mathbf{n}:=\mathbf{n}
,
\mathbf{n}\boxast\emptyset:=\emptyset\boxast\mathbf{n}:=\mathbf{n}
for every index
\mathbf{n}
. Furthermore, for given indices
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{s})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{s},\,\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{t})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{t}
with
s,t\geq 1
, we set recursively

\displaystyle\mathbf{n}\ast\mathbf{m}:=

\displaystyle\,(n_{1}).((n_{2},\dots,n_{s})\ast\mathbf{m})+(m_{1}).(\mathbf{n}\ast(m_{2},\dots,m_{t}))

\displaystyle\,+(n_{1}+m_{1}).((n_{2},\dots,n_{s})\ast(m_{2},\dots,m_{t}))

as formal sum of indices, where
().()
means the concatenation of indices. Similarly, we define the box product
\mathbf{n}\boxast\mathbf{m}
to be the part of
\mathbf{n}\ast\mathbf{m}
of smallest length.

Example 43
.

To illustrate the definition of stuffle product and box product of indices, we consider
\mathbf{n}=(1,2)
and
\mathbf{m}=(3,2)
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{n}\ast\mathbf{m}=

\displaystyle\,(1,2)\ast(3,2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(4,4)+(1,5,2)+(1,3,4)+2(4,2,2)+(3,3,2)

\displaystyle\,+(1,2,3,2)+2(1,3,2,2)+2(3,1,2,2)+(3,2,1,2)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{n}\boxast\mathbf{m}=\,(1,2)\boxast(3,2)=\,(4,4).

In the following, for
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we consider the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{z,d}:=\left\{(\mathbf{n_{1}},\mathbf{n_{2}}\boxast\boldsymbol{\ell})-(\mathbf{n_{1}}\ast\mathbf{n_{2}},\boldsymbol{\ell})\,\Bigg|\,\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{n_{1}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{s_{1}},\,\mathbf{n_{2}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{s_{2}},\,\boldsymbol{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d},\\
1\leq s_{1},s_{2}\leq d,\,|\mathbf{n_{1}}|+|\mathbf{n_{2}}|+\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\ell}=z+d}\end{subarray}\right\}\subset\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{J}_{z,d},

where
(\cdot,\cdot)
is
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinearly continued.

Lemma 44
.

For all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have
\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{K}_{z,d}\subset\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
19
.
∎

By numerical calculations (see the appendix), we conjecture that the converse inclusion is also true if
z\leq d
.

Conjecture 45
.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\leq d
. Then,

(45.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{K}_{z,d}=\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}.

We say that Conjecture
45
is true for
(z_{0},d_{0})
if (
45.1
) is true for
(z,d)=(z_{0},d_{0})
. Note the following consequence.

Lemma 46
.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\leq d
. If Conjecture
45
is true for
(z,d)
, we have

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d}\geq\binom{z+d-1}{d}.

In particular, if
z=d
additionally, then Conjecture
39
is true for
(d,d)
.

Proof.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\leq d
. We begin by noting that we have

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{K}_{z,d}=\sum\limits_{j=2}^{z}\binom{z+d-1}{d+j-1}

since
\#\mathcal{K}_{z,d}
is the number of ways one can write
z+d
as ordered sum of at least
d+2
and at most
d+\min\{z,d\}
(
=d+z
in case
z\leq d
) positive integers. Now, if Conjecture
45
is true for
(z,d)
, we obtain by (
42.1
) and (
42.2
), that

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d}={j}_{z,d}-\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}\geq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{z}\binom{z+d-1}{d+j-1}-\sum\limits_{j=2}^{z}\binom{z+d-1}{d+j-1}=\binom{z+d-1}{d}.

In case
z=d
, the right hand side is
{t}_{d,d}
, i.e., we must have equality and so, Conjecture
39
is true for
(d,d)
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The set
\mathcal{K}_{z,d}
seems to be of special interest regarding determining a basis of
\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}
as the following refinement of Conjecture
45
shows.

Conjecture 47
.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\leq d
. Then
\mathcal{K}_{z,d}
is a basis of
\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}
.

As usual, we say that Conjecture
47
is true for
(z_{0},d_{0})
if
\mathcal{K}_{z_{0},d_{0}}
is a basis of
\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z_{0},d_{0}}
. We give evidence for Conjecture
47
.

Lemma 48
.

Conjecture
47
is true for all
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
satisfying
1\leq z\leq d\leq 8
.

Proof.

For
z=1
and
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have
\mathcal{K}_{1,d}=\emptyset
and
{j}_{z,d}=d={s}_{1,d}
as we will show in Lemma
52
, i.e.,
\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{1,d}
is the trivial vector space. Hence, Conjecture
47
is true for all
(1,d)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
. For
z\geq 2
, the claim is obtained by numerical calculations, see the appendix.
∎

Note the following consequence that Conjecture
47
is a refinement of Conjecture
39
.

Lemma 49
.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\leq d
. If Conjecture
47
is true for
(z,d)
, then also Conjecture
39
is true for
(z,d)
.

Proof.

Let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\leq d
and assume that Conjecture
47
is true for
(z,d)
. By (
42.1
) and (
42.2
), then we obtain

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d}={j}_{z,d}-\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{ker}\operatorname{Box}_{z,d}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{z}\binom{z+d-1}{d+j-1}-\sum\limits_{j=2}^{z}\binom{z+d-1}{d+j-1}=\binom{z+d-1}{d},

i.e., Conjecture
39
is true for
(z,d)
.
∎

We investigate
{s}_{z,d}
in the following in more detail.

Lemma 50
.

For all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d+1}+{s}_{z+1,d}\leq{s}_{z+1,d+1}.

Proof.

Fix
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. By definition of
{s}_{z,d+1}
, there are
{s}_{z,d+1}
linearly independent linear combinations

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d+1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{z})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\qquad(1\leq j\leq{s}_{z,d+1}).

Then, the
{s}_{z,d+1}
linear combinations (
1\leq j\leq{s}_{z,d+1}
in the following) of case
(z+1,d+1)
,

(50.1)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d+1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{z})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{d},\ell_{d+1}+1)},

are linearly independent as well. Note that all occurring words
u_{\mu_{1}}\cdots u_{\mu_{d+1}}
in this linear combinations satisfy
\mu_{d+1}\geq 2
.

Now, by definition of
{s}_{z+1,d}
, there are
{s}_{z+1,d}
linear independent linear combinations

(50.2)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z+1,d}}b_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{z})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\qquad(1\leq j\leq{s}_{z+1,d}).

Considering for
1\leq j\leq{s}_{z+1,d}
the following linear combinations in case
(z+1,d+1)

(50.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z+1,d}}b_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{z})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}u_{1}\\
=&\left(\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z+1,d}}b_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{z})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\right)u_{1}+\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z+1,d}}b_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{z})\,\left(u_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{s-1})}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\right)u_{1+n_{s}}\end{split}

are linearly independent again because of (
50.2
). Furthermore, they and the ones from (
50.1
) are linearly independent since the latter ones contain words ending in
u_{\mu_{d+1}}
with
\mu_{d+1}\geq 2
while the linear independence of (
50.3
) already comes from words ending all in
u_{1}
.

Summarized, we have proven

{s}_{z,d+1}+{s}_{z+1,d}\leq{s}_{z+1,d+1}.\penalty\
∎

Remark 51
.

Assuming Conjecture
39
, the inequality in Lemma
50
is an equality if and only if
z\neq d
.

With Lemma
50
, we can now prove the following partial result towards Conjecture
39
.

Lemma 52
.

Conjecture
39
is true for all pairs
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
with
1\leq z\leq 3
.

Proof.

Note that the proof for
1\leq z\leq 2
is contained in Remark
66
. Therefore, assume
z=3
in the following. For
(z,d)\in\{(3,1),(3,2),(3,3)\}
, the claim follows from Remark
66
. Hence, consider
d\geq 4
and prove by induction (with already proven base case
d=3
) on
d
. By Lemma
50
, the induction hypothesis, and the case
z=2
of the lemma that is proven in Remark
66
, we know that

(52.1)

\displaystyle{s}_{3,d}\geq{s}_{3,d-1}+{s}_{2,d}=\binom{d+1}{2}+\binom{d+1}{1}=\binom{d+2}{2}.

Therefore, it suffices to prove
{s}_{3,d}\leq\binom{d+2}{2}
. Note that for
(z,d)=(3,d)
the number of box products spanning
{S}_{3,d}
is
\binom{d+2}{0}+\binom{d+2}{1}+\binom{d+2}{2}
. I.e., if we can show that
\binom{d+2}{2}
of those are such that the other
\binom{d+2}{0}+\binom{d+2}{1}
ones are in their
\mathbb{Q}
-span, we are done. We consider the set of
\binom{d+2}{2}
box products

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3,d}:=\left\{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{2}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d},\ u_{1}u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{d},\,\\
u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{j_{1}}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j_{1}-1},\,u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j_{2}}u_{3}u_{1}^{d-j_{2}-1},\\
u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j_{3}}u_{2}u_{1}^{j_{4}}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j_{3}-j_{4}-2}\end{subarray}\Bigg|\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j_{1}\leq d-2,\,0\leq j_{2}\leq d-2,\\
0\leq j_{3},j_{4}\leq d-2,\,j_{3}+j_{4}\leq d-2\end{subarray}\right\}.

In the following, we show that the other box products in case
(z,d)=(3,d)
are in the
\mathbb{Q}
-span of
\mathcal{R}_{3,d}
. For
0\leq j_{1}\leq d-2
, we obtain

(52.2)

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j_{1}}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j_{1}-1}=\frac{1}{2}\left((u_{1}\ast u_{1}-u_{2})\boxast u_{1}^{j_{1}}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j_{1}-1}\right)\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{R}_{3,d}

due to Lemma
19
and the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{3,d}
.
Furthermore, we have that

(52.3)

\displaystyle u_{3}\boxast u_{1}^{d}=\sum\limits_{j_{2}=0}^{d-1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j_{2}}u_{3}u_{1}^{d-j_{2}-1}-(u_{2}u_{1}+u_{1}u_{2})\boxast u_{1}^{d}

is in the
\mathbb{Q}
-span of
\mathcal{R}_{3,d}
. This implies, due to
u_{2}\ast u_{1}=u_{2}u_{1}+u_{1}u_{2}+u_{3}
and Lemma
19
, that

\displaystyle u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-1}u_{2}=\,

\displaystyle u_{2}\boxast\left(u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d}-\sum\limits_{j_{1}=0}^{d-2}u_{1}^{j_{1}}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(u_{2}u_{1}+u_{1}u_{2}+u_{3})\boxast u_{1}^{d}-\sum\limits_{j_{1}=0}^{d-2}u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{j_{1}}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j_{1}-1}\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{R}_{3,d}.

Similar to (
52.2
), one obtains now

\displaystyle u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{d-1}u_{2}\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{R}_{3,d}.

Using (
52.3
), Lemma
19
and the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{3,d}
, we get

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d}=\frac{1}{3}\left((u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{1}-u_{2}u_{1}-u_{1}u_{2})\boxast u_{1}^{d}\right)\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{R}_{3,d},

completing the claim. In particular, the lemma is proven for
z=3
.
∎

Proposition 53
.

Conjecture
39
is true for
(4,4)
and therefore, by Theorem
41
, for all pairs
(z,4)
with
z\geq 4
.

Proof.

Using Corollary
40
, we have to show
{S}_{4,4}={T}_{4,4}
. From Theorem
37
and Lemma
32
, we already have

\displaystyle u_{2}u_{2}u_{2}u_{2},\,u_{5}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{5}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{1}u_{5}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{5},\,u_{1}u_{3}u_{2}u_{2},\,u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}u_{2},

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}u_{3},\,u_{3}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{3}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{2}u_{3}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{4}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{3}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{4}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},

\displaystyle u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{4},\,u_{1}u_{1}u_{3}u_{3},\,u_{1}u_{1}u_{4}u_{2},\,u_{2}u_{1}u_{3}u_{2},\,u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}u_{3},\,u_{2}u_{3}u_{1}u_{2},\,u_{3}u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}\in{S}_{4,4}.

Hence, considering
u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}
, we obtain
u_{3}u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}\in{S}_{4,4}
, and so, by Corollary
31
,we also have
u_{2}u_{2}u_{1}u_{3}\in{S}_{4,4}
. Now, considering
u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}{j_{1}}u_{2}u_{1}^{j_{2}}u_{2}u_{1}^{j_{3}}
for
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}=2
, yields
u_{3}u_{1}u_{3}u_{1},\,u_{3}u_{1}u_{1}u_{3},\,u_{1}u_{3}u_{3}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{3}u_{1}u_{3}\in{S}_{4,4}
. Last, consider
u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j_{1}}u_{2}u_{1}^{j_{2}}u_{3}u_{1}^{j_{3}}
for
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}=1
immediately gives
u_{2}u_{1}u_{4}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{4},\,u_{1}u_{2}u_{4}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{4}\in{S}_{4,4}
, yielding, by Corollary
31
again, that
u_{4}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{4}u_{1}u_{1}u_{2},\,u_{1}u_{4}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{4}u_{1}u_{2}\in{S}_{4,4}
. Therefore,
{S}_{4,4}={T}_{4,4}
follows, completing the proof.
∎

4.3.
Abuot a possible basis of the spaces
{S}_{z,d}

After discussing the spaces
{S}_{z,d}
and its dimension, we give in this subsection a possible basis of
{S}_{z,d}
for
1\leq z\leq d
. The results were obtained using numerical calculations, a proof for Conjecture
56
is postponed to future works.

Definition 54
.

Given
1\leq z\leq d
. We define the set
\mathfrak{M}_{z,d}
of
plain basis vectors
recursively as follows:

(i)

For
z=1
, we set
\mathfrak{M}_{0,d}=\{u_{1}^{d}\}
.

(ii)

For
z>1
, we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{z,d}:=\{u_{\ell}\mathtt{W}\colon\mathtt{W}\in\mathfrak{M}_{z-\delta_{z=d},d-1},\,1\leq\ell\leq z+d-\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W})\}.

Remark 55
.

More explicitly,
\mathfrak{M}_{z,d}
is the set of
u_{\ell_{1}}\cdots u_{\ell_{d}}
with
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=j}^{d}\ell_{k}\leq\min\{z,d-j+1\}+d-j

for all
1\leq j\leq d
.

Conjecture 56
.

Let be
1\leq z\leq d
. The set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{z,d}:=\left\{u_{n_{1}}\cdots u_{n_{s}}\boxast u_{\ell_{1}}\cdots u_{\ell_{d}}\colon\begin{subarray}{c}u_{\ell_{1}}\cdots u_{\ell_{d}}\in\mathfrak{M}_{z,d},\ n_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\ (1\leq j\leq s),\\
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{s}+\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{d}=z+d,\,\ell_{1}>z-d+\delta_{n_{1}=s=1}\end{subarray}\right\}

is a basis of
{S}_{z,d}
.

Example 57
.

Consider
d=4
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{1,4}=

\displaystyle\{u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{2,4}=

\displaystyle\{u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{3,4}=

\displaystyle\left\{u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\,u_{1}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{2}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\,u_{3}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{4,4}=

\displaystyle\left\{u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{2}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{3}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{3}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{3}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{4}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\right\}.

Hence, conjecturally we have that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{1,4}=

\displaystyle\{u_{1}\boxast\ u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{2,4}=

\displaystyle\left\{u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{3,4}=

\displaystyle\left\{u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}\boxast u_{3}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{3}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{4,4}=

\displaystyle\left\{u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{3}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}\boxast u_{3}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}\boxast u_{4}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{2}\boxast u_{2}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}\boxast u_{3}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{3}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{3}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{3}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{3}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{3}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{2}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{4}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{3}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{3}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ \right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1},\ u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}\right\}

build a basis of
{S}_{1,4},\,{S}_{2,4},\,{S}_{3,4}
, and
{S}_{4,4}
, respectively.

Remark 58
.

(i)

For proving Conjecture
56
, for the case of
z=d
in particular, it is sufficient to show linear independence of
\mathfrak{B}_{z,d}
.

(ii)

Conjecture
56
is numerically verified for all
1\leq z\leq d=9
.

4.4.
Connection between the box product and the stuffle product

First, to connect the box product with the stuffle product, we introduce the maps
\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}
.

Definition 59
.

Fix
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
. We define the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear map
\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}\colon\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}\,|\,\operatorname{len}(\mathtt{W})=d\right\}\to\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
, given on generators by

\displaystyle u_{\mu_{1}}\cdots u_{\mu_{d}}

\displaystyle\longmapsto u_{\mu_{1}}u_{0}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots u_{\mu_{d}}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}.

Note the following connection of maps
\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}
with the box product.

Lemma 60
.

Let be
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}
. Furthermore, let be
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
satisfying
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
. Then,

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})=\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}).

Proof.

Using the notation as in the lemma, we note that particularly
\operatorname{depth}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})=d
. The claim immediately follows by the definition of the box product and the definition of the map
\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}
.
∎

The following Lemma
61
now connects the stuffle product with the box product. It will be the key for proving Theorem
74
below and one of the main observations for our approach to the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
.

Lemma 61
.

Let be
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}
. Furthermore, let be
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
satisfying
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
. Then,

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\right)\in\sum\limits_{1\leq s\leq\min\{z,d\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

Proof.

Let be
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d},\,\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
such that
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
and write
s^{\prime}
for the length of
\mathbf{n}
. I.e., we have,
u_{\mathbf{n}}\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{0,s^{\prime},|\mathbf{n}|}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{|\mathbf{k}|-d,d,|\mathbf{k}|+|\boldsymbol{\ell}|-d}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
. Since
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}
, we have
|\mathbf{n}|+|\boldsymbol{\ell}|=z+d
. Therefore, (
3.1
) implies, together with the assumption
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
, that

\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{n}}\ast\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{w-d-z,d+s^{\prime},w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}.

By (
3.2
), this implies now

(61.1)

\displaystyle\tau(u_{\mathbf{n}}\ast\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}))\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s^{\prime},d+s^{\prime},w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ},

yielding, since
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq\min\{z,d\}
,

(61.2)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{n}}\ast\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\right)=\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{\mathbf{n}}\ast\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}))\right)\in\sum\limits_{1\leq s\leq\min\{z,d\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

Furthermore, due to Corollary
17
, we also have

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\right)\in\sum\limits_{1\leq s\leq\min\{z,d\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

Hence, the lemma follows now from Lemma
60
.
∎

Corollary 62
.

Let be
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
satisfying
|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d
. If
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\mathcal{P}
with
\mathcal{P}
from (
30.1
), then
