\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}})\right)\in\sum\limits_{1\leq s\leq\min\{z,d\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}

for all
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
satisfying
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
.

Proof.

Let be
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
satisfying
|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d
. Furthermore, choose an index
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
arbitrary with the property
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
. Assume
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\mathcal{P}
, i.e., we have

(62.1)

\displaystyle u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}

with
a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}\in\mathbb{Q}
appropriate. Now, for all
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}
, by Lemma
61
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\right)\in\sum\limits_{1\leq s\leq\min\{z,d\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

I.e., by
\mathbb{Q}
-linearity of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}
, hence we obtain

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}})\right)=\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}\,\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\right)\in\sum\limits_{1\leq s\leq\min\{z,d\}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s,d+s,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f},

completing the claim.
∎

4.5.
Supplementary results and calculations regarding the box product

We collect in this subsection further results towards the box product that are connected to Conjecture
39
but not needed in the following. First, we refine Conjecture
39
. For this, we define for all
z,d,s_{\min}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
1\leq z\leq d
,

(62.2)

\displaystyle{S}_{z,d,s_{\min}}:=

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\mid(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d},\,\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{n})\geq s_{\min}\right\}\subset{S}_{z,d},

(62.3)

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d,s_{\min}}:=

\displaystyle\,\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}{S}_{z,d,s_{\min}}.

Conjecture 63
.

For all
z,d,s_{\min}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
1\leq z\leq d
, we have

(63.1)

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d,s_{\min}}=\binom{z+d-1}{z-s_{\min}}.

Given
(z_{0},d_{0},s_{\min,0})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
1\leq z\leq d
, we say that Conjecture
63

is true for

(z_{0},d_{0},s_{\min,0})
if (
63.1
) is true for
(z,d,s_{\min})=(z_{0},d_{0},s_{\min,0})
.

Remark 64
.

With Theorem
41
, we see that if Conjecture
39
is true for
z=d
, then the statement for
z>d
follows as well. Hence, we can view Conjecture
63
(via
s_{\min}=1
) indeed as a refinement of Conjecture
39
, despite it is a refinement for
z\leq d
only.

Remark 65
.

Conjecture
63
is true for all triples
(z,d,s_{\min})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with
1\leq z\leq d\leq 8
and
1\leq s_{\min}\leq 8
. The proof is obtained by computer algebra; for details, see the appendix. One could use the code in the appendix for verifying Conjecture
63
also for larger values of
z
and
d
. The only limit is the computing capacity and time since the code is based on computing ranks of matrices that grow exponentially in
z
and
d
.

In the next remark, we give an elementary proof, not based on numerical calculations, for the part of Lemma
42
that is needed for proving our main results of this paper.

Remark 66
.

We could verify Conjecture
39
for all pairs
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
with
1\leq d\leq 3
also without numerical calculations. For this, first, assume
d=1
and fix
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Note that
{T}_{z,1}=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{u_{z+1}\right\}
, yielding
{s}_{z,1}\leq\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}{T}_{z,1}=1
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle u_{z+1}=u_{1}\boxast u_{z}\in{S}_{z,1},

giving
{s}_{z,1}\geq 1
. Hence, Conjecture
39
is true for all pairs
(z,1)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
since

\displaystyle{s}_{z,1}=1=\binom{1+z-1}{\min\{z,1\}-1}.

Now, assume
z=1
and fix
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. In this case,
{S}_{1,d}=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d}\right\}
, i.e.,
{s}_{1,d}=1
. In particular, we have proven Conjecture
39
for
z=1
since

\displaystyle{s}_{1,d}=1=\binom{d+1-1}{\min\{1,d\}-1}.

Next, assume
d=2
and fix
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
. Note that the case
(z,d)=(1,2)
follows from the
z=1
-case we have proven. Note that
{T}_{z,2}=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{u_{a}u_{z+2-a}\mid 1\leq a\leq z+1\right\}
. A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle u_{a}u_{z+2-a}=\begin{cases}u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{a-1}u_{z+1-a},&\quad\text{if }2\leq a\leq z,\\
u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{z}-u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{z-1},&\quad\text{if }a=1,\\
u_{1}\boxast u_{z}u_{1}-u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{z-1}u_{1},&\quad\text{if }a=z+1.\end{cases}

Hence,
u_{a}u_{z+2-a}\in{S}_{z,2}
for all
1\leq a\leq z+1
, i.e.,
{S}_{z,2}={T}_{z,2}
, giving

\displaystyle{s}_{z,2}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}{T}_{z,2}=\binom{2+z-1}{\min\{z,2\}-1}

since we assumed
z\geq 2=d
. Hence, Conjecture
39
is true for all pairs
(z,2)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
.

Now, assume
z=2
and fix
d\geq 2
(since the
(z,d)=(2,1)
-case follows from the
d=1
-case of the theorem). In this case,
{S}_{2,d}
is spanned by the
d+2
box products

\displaystyle u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j-1}\quad(0\leq j\leq d-1),\quad u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d},\quad u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{d}.

Note that all but the last box product are linear independent since
u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d}
does not contain any word with letter
u_{3}
while
u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j-1}
does contain exactly one such one which is unique for fixed
j
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{d}=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{d-1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{j}u_{2}u_{1}^{d-j-1}-2u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d},

i.e.,
u_{2}\boxast u_{1}^{d}
is not linearly independent of the box products. Therefore,

\displaystyle{s}_{2,d}=d+1=\binom{d+2-1}{\min\{2,d\}-1}

since we assumed
d\geq 2
. This proves Conjecture
39
for
z=2
.

Now, assume
d=3
. Since the cases
(z,d)\in\{(1,3),(2,3)\}
follow from the case
z=1
, respectively
z=2
, that we have proven already, we may fix
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 3}
. For
z=3
, from Lemmas
32
,
33
, and
36
, we obtain
{S}_{3,3}={T}_{3,3}
, yielding, by Corollary
40
, the claim. For
z>3
, we apply Theorem
41
to obtain the remaining part for the proof that Conjecture
39
is true for all pairs
(z,3)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
from the case
z=3
.

Noting Corollary
40
, Conjecture
39
is equivalent to
{S}_{z,d}={T}_{z,d}
for all
z\geq d
. I.e., in these cases, every
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
with
\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
and
|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d
conjecturally can be written as
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of box products
u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
with
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}
. With the following lemmas, we reduce the number of such
\boldsymbol{\mu}
’s. For that, we have to show this, which can be seen as progress towards Conjecture
39
. For this, given
\mathtt{W}_{1},\mathtt{W}_{2}\in\left(\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\right)^{\ast}
, we call the box product
\mathtt{W}_{1}\boxast\mathtt{W}_{2}

non-trivial
if
1\leq\operatorname{len}(\mathtt{W}_{1})\leq\operatorname{len}(\mathtt{W}_{2})
.

Lemma 67
.

Let be
\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
for some
d\geq 1
. Then,
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
can be written as a linear combination of words ending in
u_{1}
and non-trivial box products.

Proof.

Choose
\boldsymbol{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
\mu_{d}>1
(for
\mu_{d}=1
there is nothing to prove). Then,

(67.1)

\displaystyle u_{\mu_{d}-1}\boxast u_{\mu_{1}}\cdots u_{\mu_{d-1}}u_{1}=u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+\left(u_{\mu_{d}-1}\boxast u_{\mu_{1}}\cdots u_{\mu_{d-1}}\right)u_{1},

i.e., after rearranging, one obtains the claim.
∎

Lemma 68
.

Fix
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>1}
with
z\geq d\geq 2
. If Conjecture
39
is true for
(z,d-1)
, then every
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
with
\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
and
|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d
can be written as linear combination of words ending in
u_{2}
and non-trivial box products.

Proof.

Assume
d
and
z
as in the lemma. Let be
\boldsymbol{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d
. If
\mu_{d}=2
, there is nothing to prove. If
\mu_{d}>2
, we proceed as in the proof of Lemma
67
. If
\mu_{d}=1
, by assumption and Theorem
41
, we have

\displaystyle u_{\mu_{1}}\cdots u_{\mu_{d-1}}=\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d-1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}

for appropriate
a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\in\mathbb{Q}
. Then,

\displaystyle\,\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d-1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}u_{1}=\,u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d-1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\left(u_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{s-1})}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\right)u_{1+n_{s}}.

The latter sum consists of words ending in some
u_{\mu_{d}^{\prime}}
with
\mu_{d}^{\prime}\geq 2
. However, such words can be written as linear combinations of words ending in
u_{2}
and box products, similar to the proof of Lemma
67
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 69
.

Fix
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>1}
with
z\geq d\geq 2
. If Conjecture
39
is true for
(z-1,d-1)
, then every
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
with
\boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
and
|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d
can be written as linear combination of words ending in
u_{3}
and non-trivial box products.

Proof.

Assume
d
and
z
as in the lemma. Using Lemma
68
, we only have to show that a word ending in
u_{2}
can be written as a linear combination of words ending in
u_{3}
and box products. Choose such a word
u_{\mu_{1}}\cdots u_{\mu_{d-1}}u_{2}
, i.e.,
2+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d-1}\mu_{j}=z+d
. Then, by assumption, one has

\displaystyle u_{\mu_{1}}\cdots u_{\mu_{d-1}}=\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z-1,d-1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}

for appropriate
a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\in\mathbb{Q}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\,\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z-1,d-1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\,u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}u_{2}=\,u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}+\sum\limits_{(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z-1,d-1}}a_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})\left(u_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{s-1})}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\right)u_{2+n_{s}}.

The latter sum consists of words ending in some
u_{\mu_{d}^{\prime}}
with
\mu_{d}^{\prime}\geq 3
. However, such words can be written as linear combinations of words ending in
u_{3}
and box products, similar to the proof of Lemma
67
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 70
.

Let be
\textbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{s},\boldsymbol{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
1\leq s\leq d
. Then,
u_{\textbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
can be written as linear combination of non-trivial box products
u_{\boldsymbol{n^{\prime}}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell^{\prime}}}
where
\boldsymbol{\ell^{\prime}}
ends in
1
.

Proof.

Writing
\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{d})
, we may assume
\ell_{d}>1
since for
\ell_{d}=1
there is nothing to prove. Then,

\displaystyle u_{\textbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=

\displaystyle\,u_{\textbf{n}}\boxast\left(u_{\ell_{d}-1}\boxast u_{(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{d-1},1)}-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d-1}u_{(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{j}+\ell_{d}-1,\dots,\ell_{d-1},1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(u_{\textbf{n}}\ast u_{\ell_{d}-1}\right)\boxast u_{(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{d-1},1)}-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d-1}u_{\textbf{n}}\boxast u_{(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{j}+\ell_{d}-1,\dots,\ell_{d-1},1)},

where we used Lemma
19
in the last step.
∎

A further result about the numbers
{s}_{z,d}
is the following lemma that gives a lower bound.

Lemma 71
.

For all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

{s}_{z,d}\geq\binom{z+d-2}{d-1}.\penalty\

Proof.

We prove by induction on
z+d
. For
z=1
, the claim is clear, since for all
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have
0\neq u_{1}\boxast u_{1}^{d}\in{S}_{1,d}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle{s}_{1,d}\geq 1=\binom{1+d-2}{d-1}.

For
d=1
, we have for all
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
equality by Lemma
42
. In particular, the base case
z+d=2
is proven. Now, let be
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>1}
and assume that the lemma is proven for all smaller values of
z+d
. By Lemma
50
and the induction hypothesis, we obtain

∎

\displaystyle{s}_{z,d}\geq{s}_{z,d-1}+{s}_{z-1,d}\geq\binom{z+d-3}{d-2}+\binom{z+d-3}{d-1}=\binom{z+d-2}{d-1}.

We end this subsection with some remark on Conjecture
39
that is independent of the rest of the paper.

Remark 72
.

Using basic linear algebra, we obtain the following equivalent formulation of Conjecture
39
in the cases
z\geq d
. Fix positive integers
d
and
z
with
z\geq d
. Conjecture
39
is true for the pair
(z,d)
if and only if the
\binom{z+d-1}{d-1}
expressions

(72.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\sum\limits_{(\boldsymbol{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}}\epsilon_{\boldsymbol{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\,u_{\boldsymbol{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\ \Bigg|\ \boldsymbol{\mu}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d},\,|\boldsymbol{\mu}|=z+d\right\}

are
\mathbb{Q}
-linearly independent. Here,
\epsilon_{\boldsymbol{n},\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
denotes the multiplicity of
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
in
u_{\boldsymbol{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
.

5.
Our approach to the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10

In the following, we present the approach with which one is trying to make progress in proving the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
. The general idea is to prove by induction on
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})
for
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
that
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
. This is trivial for the base case
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})=0
. Thus, we assume
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})>0
. Particularly - for proving the induction step - one has to write
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)
as a linear combination of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}^{\prime}\right)
’s with
\mathtt{W}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
and
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W}^{\prime})<\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})
. In our approach, we refine the induction step by showing that for every word
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
we can write
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)
as a linear combination of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}^{\prime}\right)
’s with
\mathtt{W}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
and
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W}^{\prime})<\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})
, or

\displaystyle\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W}^{\prime})=\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W}^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}^{\prime})<\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W})+\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W})

(see the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
). The general observation of why the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is of interest when studying Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is given in the following lemma.

Lemma 73

(Lemma
11
)
.

Fix
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. If the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for
(z,d,w)
and if Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with
z^{\prime}+d^{\prime}+w^{\prime}<z+d+w
, then Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for
(z,d,w)
. In particular, the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
implies Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
.

Proof.

Fix
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and assume that the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for
(z,d,w)
and that Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all triples
(z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
satisfying
z^{\prime}+d^{\prime}+w^{\prime}<z+d+w
. By definition of
\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
and the second part of our assumption, it follows

\displaystyle\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}=

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w-1}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}z^{\prime}+d^{\prime}=z+d-1\\
0\leq z^{\prime}\leq z\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}

\displaystyle\subset

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+d,w-1}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}+\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+d-1,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}.

Using the assumption
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
, we obtain
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{z+d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
, i.e., Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
for
(z,d,w)
.
∎

For given
z\geq d
, our approach to the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
restricts - independent of the weight
w
- to prove Conjecture
39
for the pair
(z,d)
as the following theorem shows.

Theorem 74
.

Fix
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\geq d
. If Conjecture
39
is true for the pair
(z,d)
, then for all
w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}z^{\prime}+d^{\prime}=z+d-1\\
0\leq z^{\prime}\leq z-1\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}.

In particular, the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for the triples
(z,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with
w
arbitrary.

Proof.

Fix
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\geq d
and assume that Conjecture
39
is true for
(z,d)
. This means
u_{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{P}
for all
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{z}|=z+d
. Hence, the claim follows immediately from Corollary
62
.
∎

Remark 75
.

Immediately from Theorems
41
and
74
the following statement is obtained: If Conjecture
39
is true for all
z=d
, then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z}}_{z}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z}}_{d-1}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}

for all
(z,d)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
with
z\geq d
. More precise, then we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}=\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq z\leq d-1\\
d\geq 1\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,2z+d-1}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

Remark 76
.

For
z\geq d
, our approach to Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
, and the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
, is to study Conjecture
39
in more detail. We will explain this in Section
6
. For
z<d
, this approach will not suffice since in this case, we have
{S}_{z,d}\subsetneq{T}_{z,d}
by Conjecture
39
which is numerically explicit verified for small values of
z
and
d
(see Lemma
42
). Hence, we need to extend our approach. We make do with few explicit calculations to prove our main results in Section
6
. In the outlook, Section
7
, we abstract our calculations and leave it as an open question whether this generalization is sufficient.

6.
Proof of our main results towards the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10

In this section, we first provide the proof of our main results, namely, Theorems
8
and
12
, where some particular statements are black-boxed. We deliver their proofs in Sections
6.1
,
6.2
, and
6.3
.

Proposition 77
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all
(z,2,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

Proof.

Due to case
d=2
of Lemma
42
, Conjecture
39
is true for all
(z,2)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{2}
with
z\geq 2
. Theorem
74
then implies
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,2,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,2,w}
for all
z,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z\geq 2
. Hence, it remains to prove case
z=1
. However, this follows immediately from the special case
d=2
of Corollary
28
.
∎

Proposition 78
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all
(z,3,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

Proof.

The case
z=1
is proven by Corollary
28
, the case
z=2
will follow from Theorem
82
below, and the cases
z\geq 3
are proven by the
z=3
case of Lemma
52
, Theorem
41
, and Theorem
74
.
∎

Proposition 79
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all
(z,4,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
.

Proof.

While the case
z=1
is proven by Corollary
28
, the case
z=2
will be obtained from Theorem
87
below, and the case
z=3
will be obtained from Theorem
102
below. Furthermore, the cases
z\geq 4
are proven by Proposition
53
and Theorem
74
, completing the claim.
∎

We are now able to prove one of our main theorems.

Theorem 80

(Theorem
8
)
.

Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
is true for all
(z,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with
z+d\leq 6
.

Proof.

For
z+d\leq 3
, the claim is an immediate consequence of Proposition
21
and Corollary
28
. For
z=d=2
, the claim follows by induction on
w
, using the proven claim for
z+d\leq 3
, Lemma
73
, and Proposition
77
in the induction step. Together with Proposition
21
and Corollary
28
, the claim holds now for
z+d\leq 4
. Again, inductively on
w
, the claim for
(z,d)\in\{(3,2),(2,3)\}
follows from the already proven claim for
z+d\leq 4
, Lemma
73
, and Proposition
77
(for
(z,d)=(3,2)
), and Proposition
78
(for
(z,d)=(2,3)
). Now, using Proposition
21
and Corollary
28
, the claim follows for
z+d\leq 5
. Analogously, for
(z,d)\in\{(4,2),(3,3),(2,4)\}
, the claim follows in each case inductively on
w
, where we use in the induction step the already proven claim for
z+d\leq 5
, Lemma
73
, and Proposition
77
(for
(z,d)=(4,2)
),
78
(for
(z,d)=(3,3)
), and
79
(for
(z,d)=(2,4)
), respectively. Now, using Proposition
21
and Corollary
28
, the theorem is proven for
z+d\leq 6
as well, completing the proof.
∎

Theorem
80
is the main ingredient in the proof of the next main theorem.

Theorem 81

(Theorem
12
)
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all triples of positive integers
(z,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
with
1\leq d\leq 4
.

Proof.

For
1\leq z<d\leq 4
and
w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
arbitrary, we obtain the claim from Theorem
80
. Furthermore, for
1\leq d\leq 3
,
z\geq d
and
w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
arbitrary, we obtain the claim from Corollary
62
, Lemma
42
, and Theorem
74
. For
d=z=4
and
w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
arbitrary, the claim follows from Proposition
53
and Corollary
62
. Hence, for
z\geq d=4
and
w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
arbitrary, the claim is a direct consequence of Corollary
62
and Theorem
74
, proving the theorem finally.
∎

6.1.
The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
for
(z,d,w)=(2,3,w)

Theorem 82
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all
(2,3,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
, i.e.,

(82.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

for all
k_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,z_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, for
1\leq j\leq 3
, satisfying
z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}=2
and
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+2
.

Proof.

For
k_{1}=k_{2}=k_{3}=1
and for all
z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\geq 0
satisfying
z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}=2
, (
82.1
) is true since, after using
\tau
-invariance of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{3}}\right)=\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{3}+1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{z_{3}+1}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}.

Furthermore, for
k_{2}>1
, (
82.1
) will follow from Lemma
84
, for
k_{3}>1
, (
82.1
) will follow from Lemma
85
, and for
k_{1}>1
, (
82.1
) will follow from Lemma
86
, completing the proof of the theorem.
∎

Lemma 83
.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+2
. We have

(83.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}\right),\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.53581pt\in\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w},

(83.2)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}\right)\equiv\,

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

(83.3)

\displaystyle\equiv\,-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}\right)\equiv\,

\displaystyle-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}.

In particular, for fixed
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}
, if one of the latter four formal Multiple Zeta Values is in
\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}
, (
82.1
) is true for the corresponding choice of
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}
.

Proof.

First note that (
83.1
) is a consequence of Corollaries
25
and
29
. Furthermore, after using Lemma
61
and
\tau
-invariance of formal
q
MZVs, with (
83.1
), we obtain

(83.4)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}(u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1})\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

(83.5)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}+u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

(83.6)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w},

(83.7)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}(u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1})\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

(83.8)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}+u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{3}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

(83.9)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w},

(83.10)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}(u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2})\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

(83.11)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}+u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{3}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

(83.12)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}\right)\hskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}.

We obtain (
83.2
) and (
83.3
), by comparing (
83.6
), (
83.10
), and (
83.12
).
∎

Lemma 84
.

Equation (
82.1
) is true for
k_{2}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+3
. By (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle u_{2}u_{1}\ast u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{0,5,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}.

Hence, and due to
\tau
-invariance of formal
q
MZVs, we have

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{2}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{2}u_{1})\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{2}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)+\frac{k_{1}}{k_{2}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{(k_{1}+1,k_{2},k_{3})}(u_{1}u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w},

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w},

where the last step is a consequence of Lemma
61
. Now, with Lemma
83
, (
82.1
) indeed is true for
k_{2}>1
. ∎

Lemma 85
.

Equation (
82.1
) is true for
k_{3}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+3
. By (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle u_{2}\ast u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{1,4,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}.

Hence, and due to
\tau
-invariance of formal
q
MZVs, we have

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{3}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{2})\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{3}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)+\frac{k_{2}}{k_{3}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{k_{2}}{k_{3}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)+\frac{k_{1}}{k_{3}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{(k_{1}+1,k_{2},k_{3})}(u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}.

The last step is obtained by Lemmas
61
and
84
. Hence, the lemma is proven by Lemma
83
.
∎

Hence, for proving Proposition
78
, the remaining case is
k_{2}=k_{3}=1
.

Lemma 86
.

Equation (
82.1
) is true for
k_{1}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
k_{1}>1
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+2
. Due to Lemmas
84
and
85
, we may assume
k_{1}>1
and
k_{2}=k_{3}=1
, i.e.,
w=k_{1}+4
then. By Proposition
77
, we have
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{1}u_{0}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{2,2,w-1}
and thus
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{1}u_{0}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}
. Multiplying out the latter product and using Proposition
77
, (
83.1
), and Lemma
84
, we see that

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{1}u_{0}\right)\equiv 2\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}+u_{k_{1}}u_{1}u_{0}u_{1}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w},

where the last congruence is obtained from (
83.6
). Thus, the proof of the lemma follows from Lemma
83
.
∎

6.2.
The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
for
(z,d,w)=(2,4,w)

Theorem 87
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all
(2,4,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
, i.e.,

(87.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

for all
k_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,z_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, for
1\leq j\leq 4
, satisfying
z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}+z_{4}=2
and
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+2
.

Proof.

In the case
k_{1}=k_{2}=k_{3}=k_{4}=1
, (
87.1
) is true since for all
z_{1},\dots,z_{4}\geq 0
, we have by
\tau
-invariance of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}
that

(87.2)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)=\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{4}+1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{z_{1}+1}\right)\in\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{D,W}}_{4,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}.

In the four cases
k_{i_{1}},\ k_{i_{2}},\ k_{i_{3}}>1
with pairwise distinct
i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, (
87.1
) will follow from Lemma
92
, Proposition
93
, and Proposition
94
. Furthermore, the six cases
k_{i_{1}},k_{i_{2}}>1
for distinct
i_{1},i_{2}\in\{1,2,3,4\}
(and the two other
k_{j}
’s equal
1
) then follow from Lemmas
92
,
95
,
96
, and
97
. Next, the four cases of
k_{i}>1
(
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
) (and the three other
k_{j}
’s equal
1
), will follow from Lemmas
98
,
99
,
100
, and
101
. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

In the following three lemmas, we state some congruences that are true independently of the several cases we might consider.

Lemma 88
.

Let be
k_{1},\dots,k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+\cdots+k_{4}+2
. We have

(88.1)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},\quad

(88.2)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},\quad

(88.3)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

Proof.

Note that (
88.1
) is a direct consequence of Corollary
25
, while (
88.2
) follows from Corollary
29
. Last, (
88.3
) follows from (
88.1
) and the special case
d=4
,
z=2
,
j=3
of Corollary
27
. ∎

Lemma 89
.

Let be
k_{1},\dots,k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+\cdots+k_{4}+2
. We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}0\equiv\ &\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)\\

, for (

(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})=((1),(1,1,2,1))
, for (

), we used
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})=((1),(1,1,1,2)

(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})=((2),(1,1,1,1))
. Furthermore, for (

), we used the element
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})=((1,1),(1,1,1,1))
of
\mathcal{J}_{2,4}
.
∎

Corollary 91
.

Let be
k_{1},\dots,k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+\cdots+k_{4}+3
. We have

(91.1)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

(91.2)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

(91.3)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

(91.4)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

Proof.

Adding (
90.4
) and (
90.5
), yields, applying (
88.3
),

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle-k_{2}\left(\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle k_{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

where the last step follows from (
89.4
). Hence, (
91.1
) is proven. Furthermore, (
91.2
) is deducted from (
89.2
) and (
91.1
). Now, (
91.3
) follows from (
90.3
) and (
91.2
). Since (
91.4
) is a consequence of (
91.3
) and (
88.3
), the corollary is proven.
∎

Lemma 92
.

Equation (
87.1
) is true for
k_{2},k_{4}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+4
. By (
88.1
), (
89.1
), and (
91.3
), we have

(92.1)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{1}u_{3})\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

(92.2)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle k_{4}k_{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{4}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

(92.3)

\displaystyle+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}\left(u_{1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\right)

(92.4)

\displaystyle+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{1}\left(u_{0}u_{0}u_{1}\ast u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

Now, by (
89.2
), (
89.4
), (
89.6
), and (
91.1
), the latter is, modulo
\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}
, congruent

(92.5)

\displaystyle k_{4}k_{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)-\binom{k_{3}+1}{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}+2}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)

(92.6)

\displaystyle+k_{3}k_{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)-\binom{k_{2}+1}{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+2}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}\right).

Using (
90.2
), (
90.3
), (
90.4
), and (
90.5
), the latter is, modulo
\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}
, congruent

(92.7)

\displaystyle-k_{2}k_{3}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}k_{2}k_{3}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle+k_{3}k_{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)-\frac{1}{2}k_{2}k_{3}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle k_{2}k_{3}\left(-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-65.44142pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

With (
89.2
), (
89.3
), (
89.6
), and (
91.2
), one obtains so

(92.8)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

Now, this, together with (
89.3
) and (
91.2
) imply

(92.9)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}+1}u_{k_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

Furthermore, (
90.2
) and (
92.9
) imply

(92.10)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

Note that by Lemma
88
, Corollary
91
, and the congruences in Lemma

, the claim follows. ∎

Proposition 93
.

Equation (
87.1
) is true for
k_{1},k_{3},k_{4}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
k_{1},k_{3},k_{4}>1
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+2
. For all
z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{2}+z_{3}+z_{4}=2
, using Theorem
82
in the first step and Lemma
92
additionally in the second step, we have

\displaystyle 0\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}\ast u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

Using this observation, for
z_{1}\geq 1
,
z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{1}+\cdots+z_{4}=2
, we have, using Corollary
28
in the first step due to
z_{2}+z_{3}+z_{4}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{1}}\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{1}}\ast u_{z_{4}+1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{4}+1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{z_{1}+1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proposition 94
.

Equation (
87.1
) is true for
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}>1
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+2
. Using Lemma
92
and Proposition
93
, we obtain for
z_{1},z_{2},z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{4}=2
that

\displaystyle 0\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\ast u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}\right)\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

where we used Proposition
21
and Proposition
78
for the first congruence. Now, for all
z_{1},\dots,z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{1}+\cdots+z_{4}=2
and
z_{3}>0
, we have

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{3}}\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{3}}\ast u_{z_{4}+1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{z_{1}+1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{4}+1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{z_{1}+1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Lemma 100
.

k_{2}>1
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+2
. According to Lemmas
92
,
96
,
97
, we may assume
k_{1}=k_{3}=k_{4}=1
, i.e.,
w=k_{2}+5
. First note that, by Proposition
78
and Lemma
98
, one has

(100.1)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{1}\right)\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{1}u_{1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

giving, with (
89.4
) and (
91.1
),

(100.2)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

Furthermore, by Proposition
78
for the first congruence, Corollary
28
, Proposition
78
and Lemma
98
, Equations (
89.1
), (
91.2
), and (
100.2
) for the second congruence, we obtain

(100.3)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}\ast u_{1}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{1}u_{0}\right)\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{1}u_{1}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

The remaining part of the proof follows from (
88.1
), (
88.2
), (
91.2
), (
91.1
), and Lemma
89
, immediately.
∎

Lemma 101
.

Equation (
87.1
) is true for
k_{1}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
k_{1}>1
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+2
. According to Lemma
97
, we may assume
k_{2}=k_{3}=k_{4}=1
, i.e.,
w=k_{1}+5
. For any
z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{2}+z_{3}+z_{4}=2
, we have, using Corollary
28
, Proposition
78
, and Lemmas
98
,
99
, and
100
for the third congruence,

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}\ast\tau\left(u_{z_{4}+1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{z_{2}+1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

(101.1)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

(101.2)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}.

This implies, for any
z_{1}>0,z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{1}+\cdots+z_{4}=2
, using Proposition
78
for the first congruence and, additionally, Corollary
28
for the third congruence,

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{1}}\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{1}}\ast u_{z_{4}+1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{4}+1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{z_{1}+1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{2,4,w},

completing the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.3.
The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
for
(z,d,w)=(3,4,w)

Theorem 102
.

The refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
is true for all
(3,4,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
, i.e.,

(102.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

for all integers
k_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,z_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, for
1\leq j\leq 4
, satisfying
z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}+z_{4}=3
and
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3
.

Proof.

In the case
k_{1}=k_{2}=k_{3}=k_{4}=1
, (
102.1
) is true since, by
\tau
-invariance of
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}
, for any
z_{1},\dots,z_{4}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{1}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\equiv\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{4}+1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{z_{1}+1}\right)\in\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}.

For
k_{3}>1
, (
102.1
) will follow from Lemma
106
, for
k_{4}>1
, (
102.1
) will follow from Lemma
107
, for
k_{2}>1
, (
102.1
) will follow from Lemma
108
, and for
k_{1}>1
, (
102.1
) will follow from Lemma
109
. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

First, we will consider some relations we need more than once.

and (

) is obtained from Lemma

and the direct calculation

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{p=1}^{3}(-1)^{p}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}\left(u_{1}^{p}\boxast u_{1}^{j-1}u_{4-p}u_{1}^{4-j}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}\left(u_{1}^{j-1}u_{4}u_{1}^{4-j}+u_{2}^{j-1}u_{1}u_{2}^{4-j}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

Note that (
105.6
) is a consequence of (
103.1
), (
104.3
), (
104.8
), (
104.7
), when using (
104.14
). Analogously, (
105.7
) is a consequence of (

), (
104.6
), (
104.9
), (
104.10
), using (
104.13
). Furthermore, we obtain (
105.8
) with (

), (
104.15
), and case
j=3
of (
105.1
), in a similar way, using Lemma

,

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{1}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{2})\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{2}u_{1}u_{1})\right)

(105.10)

\displaystyle-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{1}u_{2})\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{3}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(105.11)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

and we obtain (

) with (

), (
104.15
), and with case

of (

),

(105.12)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{1}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1})\right)-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{2}\boxast u_{1}u_{1}u_{2}u_{1})\right)

(105.13)

\displaystyle-\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{2}\boxast u_{2}u_{1}u_{1}u_{1})\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(105.14)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)+\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)\hskip-5.69054pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

completing the proof of the lemma.
∎

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(107.7)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

The second congruence is a consequence of the first one and (
105.7
).

Furthermore, Theorem
87
for the first congruence, Lemma
106
and (
104.5
) for the third one, give

(107.8)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{4}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{2})\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(107.9)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{4}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(107.10)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{4}}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(107.11)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

and so, applying case
j=3
of (
105.1
),

(107.12)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

Furthermore, by Theorem
87
for the first congruence and by Lemma
106
and (
104.2
) for the third one, we observe

(107.13)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{4}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{2})\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(107.14)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{4}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{3}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(107.15)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{4}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{3}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(107.16)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

This implies, using (
105.9
), and (
104.13
) for the second congruence additionally,

(107.17)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(107.18)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

Now, (
104.9
), (
107.11
), (
107.5
), (
107.17
) yield

(107.19)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(107.20)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

The second congruence is a consequence of the first one and (
105.6
). Using (
107.20
) and equations (
104.7
) and (
107.16
), we see that

(107.21)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(107.22)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

where the second congruence is implied by the first one and (
104.14
).

Combining (
104.8
), (
107.6
), (
107.1
), (
107.22
), we have

(107.23)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(107.24)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

The second congruence is a consequence of the first one and case
j=2
of (
105.1
).

Now, (
104.12
), (
107.23
), (
107.12
), (
103.1
) give

(107.25)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}+1}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(107.26)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}+1}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

The second congruence is a consequence of the first one and case
j=4
of (
105.1
) additionally. This completes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

Lemma 108
.

Equation (
102.1
) is true for
k_{2}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+4
. Note that by Theorem
87
for the first congruence and by Lemmas
106
and
107
, and equations (
106.35
) and (
106.8
) for the third congruence, we have

(108.1)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{2}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(u_{2})\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(108.2)

\displaystyle\equiv\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{2}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{1}u_{0}\ast u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(108.3)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{3}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(108.4)

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-36.98857pt\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

By (
104.1
) and (
106.37
), this yields

(108.5)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

leading to, by using (
105.7
) and then (
104.13
),

(108.6)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(108.7)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

Combining (
104.8
), (
108.5
), (
106.36
), and (
106.34
), we obtain

(108.8)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w},

(108.9)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

The second congruence is a consequence of the first one and case
j=2
of (
105.1
).

Furthermore, combining (
104.9
), (
108.4
), (
106.32
), and (
106.18
), we obtain

(108.10)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}u_{k_{3}}u_{k_{4}}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

(108.11)

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}u_{k_{2}+1}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}\right)\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

The second congruence is a consequence of the first one and case
j=3
of (
105.1
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 109
.

Equation (
102.1
) is true for
k_{1}>1
.

Proof.

Let be
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
k_{1}>1
and write
w=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3
. Using Proposition
78
for the first congruence and Lemmas
106
,
107
,
108
afterwards, for all
z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{2}+z_{3}+z_{4}=3
, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}\ast u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

(109.1)

\displaystyle\equiv\ \zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

Now, choose
z_{1}\geq 1,z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}\geq 0
with
z_{1}+\cdots+z_{4}=3
. Then, we obtain by Theorem
87
(in case
z_{1}=1
), Corollary
28
(in case
z_{1}=2
), and (
109.1
),

\displaystyle 0\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{1}}\ast\tau\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{1}}\ast u_{z_{4}+1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{z_{4}+1}u_{0}^{k_{4}-1}u_{z_{3}+1}u_{0}^{k_{3}-1}u_{z_{2}+1}u_{0}^{k_{2}-1}u_{z_{1}+1}u_{0}^{k_{1}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}

\displaystyle\equiv\

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}u_{k_{4}}u_{0}^{z_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\operatorname{F}_{3,4,w}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

7.
Conclusion and outlook

With
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
for all
(z,d,w)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}
(the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
), we gave a refinement of Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
and proved several cases. For
z\geq d
, we gave a strategy for a general proof. Furthermore, for
z<d
, we were also able to prove the cases
1\leq d\leq 4
. One can generalize our approach as described in the following paragraph.

Approach to the refined Bachmann Conjecture
10
in case
z<d
.

We fix positive integers
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z<d
in the following and assume throughout the whole paragraph that

(109.2)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{\tilde{z},\tilde{d},\tilde{w}}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{\tilde{z},\tilde{d},\tilde{w}}

for
\tilde{z}\leq z,\,\tilde{d}<d,\ \tilde{w}<w
is proven already. Note that the approach from case
z\geq d
will not suffice for the case
z<d
since
{S}_{z,d}\subsetneq{T}_{z,d}
in this case by Conjecture
39
. Therefore, we extend this approach as follows. Fix throughout this paragraph
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
. Besides

\displaystyle S^{(1)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}:=\left\{\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u_{\mathbf{n}}\boxast u_{\boldsymbol{\ell}})\right)\mid(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}\right\}\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}

(the inclusion follows from Lemma
61
), we consider

\displaystyle S^{(2)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}:=\left\{\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(\tau(\mathtt{W}_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}})\ast\tau(\mathtt{W}_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}))\right)\Bigg|\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d},\,\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{n})},\,|\mathbf{m}|\leq\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{n})+d-z,\\
\mathbf{k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d},\,k_{j}\geq k_{j}^{\prime}\geq 1\ (1\leq j\leq d),\\
|\mathbf{m}|+|\mathbf{k^{\prime}}|=s+|\mathbf{k}|,\,\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}})+\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\mathbf{k^{\prime}}})=w\end{subarray}\right\},

where

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}}:=u_{m_{1}}u_{0}^{n_{s}-1}\cdots u_{m_{s}}u_{0}^{n_{1}-1},\quad\mathtt{W}_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}=u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{0}^{\ell_{d}-1}\cdots u_{k_{d}^{\prime}}u_{0}^{\ell_{1}-1}.

Remark 110
.

Note that we have
S^{(1)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}\subset S^{(2)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}
for all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z<d
and
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
.

Furthermore, we consider

\displaystyle S^{(3)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}:=\left\{\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\sigma(k_{1})}u_{0}^{e_{1}}\cdots u_{\sigma(k_{s^{\prime}})}u_{0}^{e_{s^{\prime}}}\ast u_{\sigma(k_{s^{\prime}+1})}u_{0}^{e_{s^{\prime}+1}}\cdots u_{\sigma(k_{d})}u_{0}^{e_{d}}\right)\Bigg|\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k}},\,1\leq s^{\prime}\leq d-1,\\
\mathbf{e}=(e_{1},\dots,e_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{d},\,|\mathbf{e}|=z\end{subarray}\right\},

where
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k}}
is the set of permutations on
\{k_{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq d\}
.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
61
, we can show the following.

Lemma 111
.

Fix
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. For all
(\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\ell})\in\mathcal{J}_{z,d}
,
\mathbf{k},\,\mathbf{k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
, and
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{s}
, where
s=\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{n})
, satisfying
|\mathbf{k}|=w-z
,
|\mathbf{m}|\leq\operatorname{len}(\mathbf{n})+d-z
and
k_{j}\geq k_{j}^{\prime}\geq 1
for all
1\leq j\leq d,\ |\mathbf{m}|+|\mathbf{k^{\prime}}|=s+|\mathbf{k}|,\ \operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}})+\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W}_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\mathbf{k^{\prime}}})=w
, we have

(111.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\tau(\tau(\mathtt{W}_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}})\ast\tau(\mathtt{W}_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}))\right)\in\sum\limits_{1\leq s^{\prime}\leq s}\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z-s^{\prime},d+s^{\prime},w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}.

In particular, we have
S^{(2)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
.

Let us consider an example for illustration of Lemma
111
.

Example 112
.

Denote
w=k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}+k_{3}^{\prime}+2
in the following and choose

\displaystyle\mathbf{n}=(1),\quad\mathbf{m}=(2),\quad\boldsymbol{\ell}=(1,1,1),\quad\mathbf{k^{\prime}}=(k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2}^{\prime},k_{3}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}

in the notation of Lemma
111
. First, we see that
\mathtt{W}_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}}\ast\mathtt{W}_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}=u_{2}\ast u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{F}
, where
\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{0,4,w}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}+\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{1,3,w-1}\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathcal{U}\rangle^{\circ}
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\,\tau(\tau(u_{2})\ast\tau(u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\tau\left(u_{1}u_{0}\ast u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}^{\prime}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\,\tau\left(k_{3}^{\prime}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}^{\prime}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}^{\prime}-1}+k_{2}^{\prime}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}^{\prime}-1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+k_{2}^{\prime}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}^{\prime}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{1}^{\prime}-1}+k_{1}^{\prime}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{3}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+k_{1}^{\prime}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}^{\prime}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{2}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}+k_{1}^{\prime}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{3}^{\prime}-1}u_{1}u_{0}^{k_{2}^{\prime}-1}u_{2}u_{0}^{k_{1}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\mod\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\,k_{3}^{\prime}u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}+1}u_{0}+k_{2}^{\prime}u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}+1}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}u_{0}+k_{2}^{\prime}u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+k_{1}^{\prime}u_{k_{1}^{\prime}+1}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}u_{0}+k_{1}^{\prime}u_{k_{1}^{\prime}+1}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}+k_{1}^{\prime}u_{k_{1}^{\prime}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mod\mathcal{F}.

Hence,

\displaystyle k_{3}^{\prime}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}+1}u_{0}\right)+k_{2}^{\prime}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}+1}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}u_{0}\right)+k_{2}^{\prime}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}^{\prime}}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle+k_{1}^{\prime}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}^{\prime}+1}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}u_{0}\right)+k_{1}^{\prime}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}^{\prime}+1}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{0}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}\right)+k_{1}^{\prime}\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{k_{1}^{\prime}+1}u_{0}u_{k_{2}^{\prime}}u_{k_{3}^{\prime}}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{2,3,w}.

Compared to the linear combinations in
S^{(1)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}
, it stands out that the latter linear combination is not a linear combination of words with the same multiplicity and the same non-
u_{0}
letters in the same order. Nevertheless, all occurring words
u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{0}^{z_{2}}u_{k_{3}}u_{0}^{z_{3}}
satisfy
k_{j}\geq k_{j}^{\prime}
and
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(k_{j}-k_{j}^{\prime})=1=|\mathbf{m}|-s=d-z
.

Furthermore, we have the following.

Lemma 113
.

Fix
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z<d
and assume that
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime}}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime}}
is proven already for
z^{\prime}\leq z,\,d^{\prime}<d,\,w^{\prime}<w
. Then, for every index
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
and for all permutations
\sigma
on
\{k_{1},\dots,k_{d}\}
,
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq d-1
, and
\mathbf{e}=(e_{1},\dots,e_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{d}
satisfying
|\mathbf{e}|=z
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(u_{\sigma(k_{1})}u_{0}^{e_{1}}\cdots u_{\sigma(k_{s^{\prime}})}u_{0}^{e_{s^{\prime}}}\ast u_{\sigma(k_{s^{\prime}+1})}u_{0}^{e_{s^{\prime}+1}}\cdots u_{\sigma(k_{d})}u_{0}^{e_{d}}\right)\in\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}.

In particular, we have
S^{(3)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
.

With the proofs of Theorems
8
and
12
, we gave evidence for the following conjecture for
d\leq 4
.

Conjecture 114
.

Fix
z,d,w\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
with
z<d
and assume that
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime}}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z^{\prime},d^{\prime},w^{\prime}}
is proven already for all
z^{\prime}\leq z,\,d^{\prime}<d,\,w^{\prime}<w
. Then, for every
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
and for every word
\mathtt{W}=u_{k_{1}}u_{0}^{z_{1}}\cdots u_{k_{d}}u_{0}^{z_{d}}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
satisfying
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})=z
,
\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W})=d
, and
\operatorname{wt}(\mathtt{W})=w
, we have

(114.1)

\displaystyle\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left(S^{(2)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}\cup S^{(3)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}\right)+\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}.

In particular, then we have
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
.

Remark 115
.

Note that the inclusion in (
114.1
) follows from Lemmas
111
and
113
.

Remark 116
.

We can refine our approach to Conjecture
114
as follows. First, we will use for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{d}
satisfying
\#\{k_{j}>1\}\geq d-z
the linear combinations from
S^{(2)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}
only to show (
114.1
). For the remaining cases, we then may assume without loss of generality that
\#\{k_{j}=1\}\geq z
and use both,
S^{(2)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}
and
S^{(3)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}
to prove (
114.1
). More precise, we consider the cases of
j_{0}:=\#\{k_{j}=1\}
with increasing
j_{0}\geq z
. The intuitive reason for this is that, for given
j_{0}
, on the one hand we may assume that the cases for smaller values of
j_{0}
are proven, making the linear combinations from
S^{(2)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}
easier to handle since parts of them are in
\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
already. On the other hand, the more entries of
\mathbf{k}
are the same (for our purposes: one), the less formal Multiple Zeta Values of different words occur in the linear combinations from
S^{(3)}_{z,d,\mathbf{k}}
.

Conclusion.

For
z<d
, our strategy also works in the small cases
1\leq d\leq 4
as shown, but there is still much to do for the general proof. More concretely, we conclude with the following open questions:

(i)

How can one prove Conjecture
39
in general?

(ii)

Conjecturally, Conjecture
39
can be proven via induction on
z
,
d
, or
z+d
.

(iii)

Regarding Conjecture
39
, we conjecturally have
{s}_{z,d}={s}_{d,z}
for all
z,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Can one prove this equality?

(iv)

How to prove Conjecture
47
in general?

(v)

How can one prove
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
for
z<d
in general?

(vi)

Similar to Proposition
21
, our approach for showing
\operatorname{Fil}^{\mathrm{Z,D,W}}_{z,d,w}\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f}\subset\operatorname{F}_{z,d,w}
is suitable to obtain for all words
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
an explicit formula
\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathtt{W}\right)=\zeta_{q}^{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathcal{L}\right)
, where
\mathcal{L}
is a linear combination of products of elements in
\mathcal{Z}_{q}^{f,\circ}
. With some engagement following our calculations, this already can be done now for all words
\mathtt{W}\in\mathcal{U}^{\ast,\circ}
satisfying
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})+\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W})\leq 6
. What do they look like? Can one find some systematics such that one can derive such formulas also for
\operatorname{zero}(\mathtt{W})+\operatorname{depth}(\mathtt{W})>6
(which would prove Bachmann’s Conjecture
4
in particular)?

The numerical calculations in the paper were done using Python. In this appendix, the original source code is presented.

A.2.
The box product

In this section, we implement the box product as linear combination of words
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\left(\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\right)^{\ast}
. Furthermore, for a set of box products, we implement the adjacency matrix whichs rows will correspond to the linear combinations and the columns to the words
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, i.e., the entries are the coefficient of a word in a linear combination of box products.

We begin with the box product.

Function 121
.

The function

returns
u_{\verb|index1|}\boxast u_{\verb|index2|}
as follows. It returns a dictionary
D
containing as keys the indices
ind
satisfying that
u_{\verb|ind|}
occurs in the box product
u_{\verb|index1|}\boxast u_{\verb|index2|}
with multiplicity
\neq 0
; the value
D[ind]
then is the multiplicity of
u_{\verb|ind|}
in
u_{\verb|index1|}\boxast u_{\verb|index2|}
.

A.3.
Dimension of spaces spanned by box products

We considered in the paper the dimension of spaces spanned by several box products (in particular,
{S}_{z,d}
). Numerically, we will obtain such dimensions as the rank of the coefficient matrix of the box products that span the space we consider, interpreted as linear combination of words
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\left(\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\right)^{\ast}
. For this, we introduce the function
MATR
.

Function 123
.

The function
Dim(P)
takes a list
P
of box products, given in shape of
BOX(index1,index2)
, and returns the dimension of the space they span. This is done via computing the rank of the coefficient matrix (as list of lists) of these box products with rows corresponding to the box products, columns corresponding to the coefficient of words
u_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in\left(\mathcal{U}\backslash\{u_{0}\}\right)^{\ast}
.

⬇

1

(
P
):

2

M

=

[]

3

for

prod

in

P
:

4

I

=

[]

5

for

index

in

prod
:

6

I
.
append
(
prod
[
index
])

7

M
.
append
(
I
)

8

rk

=

np
.
linalg
.
matrix_rank
(
M
)

9

return
(
rk
)
